the programme of activities of the french presidency of the council of the european union (eu), entitled recovery, strength and a sense of belonging, expresses a commitment to economic recovery, enabling the successful ecological and digital transitions, the defence and promotion of european values and interests and the importance of building and developing a common european vision. as regards the tourism sector, it stresses the need to recover and strengthen its resilience to future crises. it also has as an objective the defence of the outermost regions and of article 349 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union (tfeu), which allows european standards to be adapted to these regions and ensures that their characteristics and constraints, as well as their full potential, are respected. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on tourism and will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's policy on tourism. to achieve this ambition, the outermost regions, given their economic dependence on the tourism sector, must be integrated into this vision and the priorities for action.
mr president, the regulation of digital services is a matter of urgency. every day, users around the world turn to digital platforms where they interact and trust personal information. if there is no regulation, it is the big corporations that continue to set the rules and put profits ahead of data protection and rights. this is already the case today, with the use of increasingly sophisticated techniques of profiling and collecting personal data. that is why any regulation must be serious and this has been half-way on essential issues. the main one is targeted advertising. in 2020 alone, google made $147 billion and facebook made $84 billion in advertising, thanks largely to developing tools that allow them to identify their target consumers, which they use to increase their profits. this proposal could and should have gone much further. increasing transparency is not enough. mechanisms need to be put in place to eliminate targeted advertising and all that is associated with it. the way this proposal is coming along is to support a business model that is incompatible with human rights. in addition, we should also have implemented limitations on the protection of freedom of the press and freedom of expression and also on the protection of the cultural and creative sectors. freedom of the press and access to culture are key dimensions of any democracy. our rights and freedoms cannot be for sale.
mr president, there are many good things in this digital services act, but it still leaves too much leverage to governments that fear the freedom of expression and cannot refrain from censorship. do i need to remind you that pablo hasel, a singer, is in prison for his lyrics? or about the spanish obsession to imprison josé arenas valtonyc, who dared to sing that the bourbons are thieves? spanish freedom of expression is different indeed. when it comes to online content, spain is addicted to censorship. they block hundreds of websites promoting the catalan referendum, they force the removal of apps that were used by activists, and they go as far as blocking the website of women on web, an organisation that counsels about abortion. yes, freedom of expression is endangered in the eu, and this act does not go far enough to protect it.
mr president, madam executive vice-president of the european commission, commissioner, first of all i would like to congratulate the rapporteur, mrs schaldemose, and also the alternative rapporteur of my group, the epp, arba kokalari, on the great work they have done, and likewise the other rapporteurs involved on their spirit of cooperation: i would like to highlight the great work of consensus that has been achieved between all the groups. i am glad that the european commission has put forward the proposal for a digital services law, which is certainly a step in the right direction: this proposal was very necessary to adapt european legislation to the new digital reality. i think it comes out of this parliament strengthened and well-positioned for the tripartite dialogues, especially since we have already discussed the various sensitivities of the different political groups represented in this house and have reached a great deal of consensus. this act is a regulation designed to create a safer online environment, while promoting innovation in the digital domain in the european union. the proposal updates the 2000 e-commerce directive: over the past 20 years, the world has changed radically with the emergence of new digital services. legislative adaptation was and is therefore urgently needed to address these new challenges. no one questions the advances these services have brought to our societies; however, there is also a growing need for increased online security. the new obligations that this digital services act establishes go precisely in this direction: we want to protect our citizens and companies from the abuses of the internet giants. to this end, the measures provided for in this act are based on a fundamental principle, 'what is illegal offline must also be illegal online', a principle that is applied through transparency rules. all this is key to the fight against impunity that currently exists on the internet and that affects our most vulnerable citizens and companies, especially minors and our smes. i would also like to stress that we have been particularly careful about the type of obligations imposed on smes, since, because of their limited resources, some of them would not be able to meet them: it is a question of simplifying their lives, not of adding bureaucratic and administrative procedures. we will have to continue working to complete this legislation, which is only a first step: with this law we lay the foundations of what security in the digital environment should be, but there are still many challenges to be addressed. it is a comprehensive and transversal legislation with which we are laying down the foundations of digital regulation in the european union, that is, it sets out basic rules for all digital content, products, services and activities based on a principle of horizontality. we certainly need further initiatives to address the specific challenges and complete robust legislation in line with our european values: this week we will adopt parliament's position on this act, and i encourage the negotiations in the tripartite dialogue to continue in the spirit of cooperation.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen of the commission, the legislation on the digital giants is an essential subject and the dsa we are studying today is undoubtedly a step forward for europe. unfortunately, despite our many warnings, you were slow to implement it. you have allowed for decades, in the name of the free market, the american giants to totally occupy our societies and our lives. and you're taking measures against them today that would have been more useful if we were twenty or thirty years behind. because today the 20 biggest tech companies are outside europe and you are responsible for that. these american giants dominate us, they crush us and replacement becomes a necessity. so our goal is to find balance and independence. if we want to win the war against the fatf, if we want to guarantee the security of europeans' data, if we want to protect their privacy, if we want to develop the potential of our own companies and ensure the european union's digital sovereignty, we can no longer apply your ideological logic of openness and free competition. i have tried to express this to you in the more than 200 amendments i have tabled, because we have an absolute duty to give priority to and encourage the development of our own european companies, to the detriment of foreign companies. to do that, the digital giants must comply with their legal, tax and regulatory obligations and we must stop, you must stop, all complacency towards gafam and other batx. commissioner breton, you have opened the way by promising that european data will be hosted only within the european union. but you have broken your word and disappointed the expectations of tens of millions of european users. so it is time for you to put words into action and for the digital challenge, the digital sovereignty that emmanuel macron mentioned this morning, to be neither a slogan nor just another communication operation, but a real political objective.
mr president, digitisation processes and digital services have great potential for development in various spheres of human activity, including the economy. but reality has already shown that this means has served to increase the exploitation of workers or the phenomena of alienation and social and behavioural conditioning of individuals in favour of profits and the dominance of large companies and multinationals. the legislative act on digital services which we are discussing here today is nothing more than a step in the further development of the digital single market, or, in other words, in the defence of the liberalisation of the digital market. the approach to regulating online platforms, which promote monopolies and strengthen the market power of multinational companies, which the digital single market serves, does not answer fundamental questions and does not compromise the interests of the digital giant. with this regulation, the european union develops another piece of supranational legislation that aims to remove the power of member states to control the digital platforms operating on their territory. the proposal is not aimed at defending the general interest of the public domain of the sector, nor at promoting free access by the public to content, access to the creation, enjoyment and free sharing of culture, rejecting the logic of commodification of culture, of attacking multilingualism or of reinforcing a greater cultural colonization. the creation and proliferation of a number of so-called independent entities is intended to remove the role of the states and their national authorities, without any desire to guarantee the independence of these entities from the major economic interests. the member states already have bodies with competence, for example, for administrative and judicial assessment of illegal content, the scope of which should be extended to the digital level, keeping the process within the sphere of the sovereignty of the states.

madam president, commissioner, in political speeches animal welfare in the european union tends to be characterised as one of the best in the world. however, we must be careful when expressing this pride because in most cases, animals that are exploited by the intensive livestock sector do not have a dignified life, quite the opposite. in livestock farming, these animals are not free to express their natural instinct, they live trapped in cages and in the whole process, from breeding, transportation, to their death, they are treated as mere commodities. and the report we are voting on tomorrow should portray this - the deplorable living conditions we subject these animals to in livestock farming - and do so in a realistic, factual and scientifically based way. unfortunately, against the wishes of the greens, this report focuses on aspects such as competitiveness in the livestock sector and the economic consequences of investing in better animal welfare standards. yes, analysing these aspects is important, but it is not central to a report whose mandate is to analyse the shortcomings in the implementation of animal welfare legislation. and it is important to remember: this report is not about farmers, it is about the animals that are in charge of these farmers and how we have failed them in terms of protection and diligence. furthermore, and in an incomprehensible way, this report states that the production of foie gras respects animal welfare. it considers that the constant violations of european legislation are only sporadic cases, rejects the mandatory labelling of food products indicating animal welfare standards and, in an attempt to protect the economic interests of livestock farmers and contrary to science, does not recognize that a reduction in meat consumption and the number of animals per farm is urgent. for all this, ladies and gentlemen, and if we really care about these animals, i ask you to support tomorrow the alternative resolution of the committee on the environment, which portrays much more faithfully the problems in the implementation of animal welfare legislation and to support the amendments of the greens to ensure that the agriculture report is in the end less bad.
the committee on budgets madam president, taxation is on the agenda. there are few dimensions of a country's sovereignty that say as much about the relationship between state and citizens. what taxes do we pay? how much are we paying? how do we pay for it? and these are the first questions that come to mind when we look at ourselves. and when we look at our countries, we see others: what tax burden is fair for citizens? what level of taxation is appropriate to promote economic growth and the competitiveness of enterprises? how do we effectively combat tax fraud and evasion? if these questions are essential for citizens, the answers are essential for states. in an increasingly globalised, digitalised and integrated economy, the challenges facing tax policy are increasingly demanding and complex. and the union is a privileged platform for debate and cooperation between member states. but our union is faced with many different realities. while some countries are focusing on freeing up resources for citizens and businesses, others are burdening taxpayers with excessive tax burdens. often, those countries which burden their taxpayers are the most reluctant to introduce tax reforms and, indeed, to introduce genuine economic reforms which are conducive to growth and employment. in the matter of taxation, everyone's idea of the role of the state is clear. and the choice is this: either a giant state that feeds off the tax burden it imposes on its taxpayers, or a sufficient state that relies on the initiative of its citizens and the innovation of its companies. this report, which is the result of the remarkable work of our colleague markus ferber, comes at the right time, as we prepare to discuss and decide on major changes in corporate taxation following the international agreement reached at oecd level. and it starts from an obvious assumption: tax reforms have a significant impact on the economies of states. and what does that mean for us in our union? first, that when we talk about taxation, sovereign power lies with the member states and the union has limited legislative powers, concentrating more on a coordinating role. but this coordinating role is essential in the context of an open internal market. when tax policies are poorly coordinated, smes are the first victims, because they cannot afford to have legal or compliance departments as large as the complexity of 27 different systems requires. and poor coordination also opens the door to opportunities for fraud, tax evasion or aggressive tax planning. this context demonstrates the need for a commission committed to leading this cooperation. and in what areas? the commission has also been active in the field of taxation, in the harmonization of tax bases, in the fearless tackling of the debt bias in corporate finance, in the creation of a clear context for tax incentives for research and development, and in the launch of a panel for the evaluation and comparison of national tax policies to promote good practice. these are three clear examples of what we are asking the european commission to do. let us therefore move forward with confidence and commitment to the reforms that our states need and our taxpayers demand.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, hundreds of thousands of citizens are awaiting the outcome of this report with great expectation: patients whose access to timely diagnosis and appropriate treatment is crucial to their survival, whether they are in strasbourg, brussels or madeira, an outermost region; fellow healthcare professionals who want to provide patients with the best possible care; researchers, who are waiting for better data to promote more and better studies and find the answers they need; families and carers who struggle every day to ensure the quality of life for their loved ones; the survivors, who want to go on with a normal life, without any economic or social constraint, guaranteed by the right to be forgotten; and finally, citizens, all citizens, from the oldest to the youngest, including their organizations, count on us, their representatives, to help improve and save lives. the aim of this report is that, by our vote and our action, we can prevent cancer from becoming a fatality. thank you to everyone who contributed to a robust report, especially the rapporteurs and the shadow rapporteurs, based on the best scientific evidence.
madam president, as has already been said, today is the international day of children with cancer and we therefore have a great responsibility to strengthen the european strategy to combat cancer, because the union that europeans want is a europe on which they can rely, especially in the most difficult times. a europe that finds solutions, a europe that solves problems. in the fight against cancer, we need to improve local responses, reduce inequalities and strengthen cooperation between member states. a europe that leaves no one behind must be a europe where patients have the right to access the best care available, regardless of the member state in which they reside. the recommendations made in this report are quite comprehensive and allow to correct some gaps in the strategy put forward by the european commission. the initial approach to paediatric and juvenile cancer was deficient, and so we sought to improve the document with proposals targeting the rare cancers that typically affect these age groups. we want to strengthen research in targeted medicine, improve palliative cancer care and facilitate access for children and young people to cross-border clinical trials. ladies and gentlemen, in a europe where health is a fundamental right, we have no time to lose.
madam president, commissioner, improving the outcome of the fight against cancer is within our reach and requires confidence and determination. trust in the advances in science that have allowed more and more patients to be treated successfully, leading to the cure and control of the disease. we therefore need to invest more in the scientific system. determination to extend the benefits of scientific progress to all europeans. to do this we need more europe in health policy, in effect making the european health union a reality. we need more europe in the fight against smoking, not giving in to the offensive of e-cigarettes, heated tobacco or new tobacco products which have only one objective: to perpetuate consumption among the young. we need to develop a monitoring system for the measures we propose which will allow us to make real progress. this report by the european parliament is a decisive contribution. i hope the commission will listen to the appeal of the citizens of europe.

member of the commission. – mr president, as the interventions from most political groups have highlighted, we urgently need to make our food systems sustainable and resilient. extreme weather phenomena, such as this drought, do not only affect the agricultural production and impact the livelihood of millions of eu citizens who live in rural areas, but are also intimately interlinked with our common fight against climate change, our concerns about soil erosion in many european regions, the impact on biodiversity and water supply. within the framework of the common agricultural policy, the lines of action under which the eu may currently provide support, both under the european agricultural guarantee fund (eagf) and the european agricultural fund for rural development (eafrd), have shown their positive effects and should be implemented to help the affected farmers. the new cap 2023—2027 strengthens the tools available, and the commission services have encouraged member states to use them to the fullest extent in their draft cap strategic plans, considering that, in the future, more extreme weather conditions will be ushered in by the climate crisis. regulation (eu) 2021/2115 includes an array of measures benefiting from eu support, of which risk management tools allowing member states the possibility to pay a farmer for the farmer’s contribution to a risk management tool, helping active farmers manage production and income risks related to their agricultural activity that are outside their control, interventions in the fruit and vegetable sector, the hop sector, the olive oil and table olive sector and the other sectors, and article 47 providing support for harvest and production insurance that contributes to safeguarding producers’ incomes where there are losses as a consequence of natural disasters, adverse climatic events, diseases or pest infestations. union financial assistance to the wine sector, article 59. due attention will be paid to how these possibilities for eu support have been considered under the spanish and portuguese cap strategic plans for 2023—2027, which were submitted in december 2021 and are currently under the analysis of the commission services. as i stated earlier, we need to adapt to climate change. this is a call to all member states that you’ll see more and more severe droughts. make the most of the new cap strategic plans to shift to less water—intensive crops and other measures recommended by the commission, and make sure that irrigation projects comply with the safeguards requested by the commission.
the debate is closed. written declarations (rule 171)
the iberian peninsula is experiencing one of the worst winter droughts in decades. reserves of water in spain and portugal are facing their worst figures in years and there are no forecasts of rain in the coming weeks. between 1 october and 1 february, rainfall in spain was 36 per cent less than the 1981-2010 measure. climate change experts warn that warming will lead to more evapotranspiration in the mediterranean region and that the available water reserves will be smaller, with situations like the current one recurring more frequently. in addition, the risk of fires will be higher and the harvests will suffer greater impacts. the socialists in this parliament are in favour of the need for eu support, cooperation between member states, efficient and rational management of water resources, the albufera convention and a european strategy on desertification. we also work to ensure that the most vulnerable and poorest populations are not affected any more and we oppose the unfeasible absorption target assigned to spain in the new proposal for the utcuts regulation, which does not take into account the impacts of climate change.
portugal is experiencing a climate emergency due to the drought that has been occurring in recent months and is likely to worsen. according to information from the portuguese institute of the sea and the atmosphere (ipma), january 2022 was the sixth driest month in a period of 90 years and the second worst since 2000, with about 35% of the territory suffering from severe drought and 12% from extreme drought. droughts and other extreme weather events pose considerable risks to human health, agriculture, biodiversity, water supply and hydropower generation. robust mechanisms need to be established or activated to combat such phenomena at european level, but with a clear focus on the circumstances of each country, such as those occurring in portugal and spain. this appropriation is intended to cover the financing of the costs of the european schools for the period 2014-2020 in accordance with the school leaving leave scheme. it is absolutely imperative that we find solutions that prevent us from reaching a point of no return, for the sake of our future.
in writing. the drought situation in portugal is very worrying and requires support and substantive measures to deal with the consequences that are already anticipated, from supply for human consumption, agricultural production and hydroelectric power to the tourism sector. in agriculture, for example, the impact on farms, requiring the use of irrigation, increases production costs, in the context of rising other factors such as energy and fuels, crushing prices in production and reducing incomes. in the cap debate we have proposed a public agricultural insurance funded from the union budget which would provide a minimum level of protection for all farmers in the member states. we believe that this would be a way of guaranteeing a minimum income to farmers affected by extreme weather events, other natural or man-made disasters, including forest fires, diseases and pests. the existence of this insurance would not prejudice the existence of other insurance schemes, specific to certain sectors, and would ensure a minimum protection for farmers. in the current situation, where mitigation and adaptation measures are still lacking, the creation of such insurance would be a wise move. unfortunately, this proposal of ours has been rejected by the majority in this parliament.
mr. chairman, please. commissioners, i would like to thank you for your kind words. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community. every year, 120,000 europeans get cancer from their jobs. it's the plumber who's inhaling asbestos and getting lung cancer. it is the carpenters who are exposed to uv radiation and get skin cancer and the veterinary nurses who take x-rays of our pets and get thyroid cancer and many other professional groups who work with substances or in environments that increase their risk of cancer and 80 000 of them die. it's mothers, fathers, brothers and sisters dying because they do their jobs. each case is tragic, but the whole picture is decidedly scandalous. it's time we start scaling up our ambitions. it's time we used some of the tools we know work. it's time we beat cancer in the workplace, too. our decisions today are an important step in the right direction. no one should get cancer from going to work, and in my eyes, one cancer patient is one too many.

cohesion policy is the main investment policy of the european union. in addition to ensuring the reduction of economic, social and territorial disparities, it allows for strategic investments that would otherwise be difficult or time-consuming to implement, such as complementary support to eu regions, such as the outermost regions, which allows for their development and the continuous reduction of inequalities. the commission should also be able to assess the impact of the measures taken on the union's financial interests, in particular on the budgetary position of the union. respect for the objectives of the paris agreement, guided by the european green deal, and the objective of energy autonomy for europe should also be a priority, in partnership with local authorities, institutions and civil society in the preparation of cohesion policy programmes from the outset. i welcome the intention of the european commission, and in particular commissioner elisa ferreira, to make the cohesion policy regulations more flexible in order to allow immediate funding and support for cities and regions hosting refugees and also to finance support for ukrainian refugees.
mr president, the golden visas operate on the basis of an indecent principle: the principle that the rights that come with nationality can be bought, the principle that people who choose our countries to work, often in low-paid jobs, can wait years to get the nationality and the rights associated with it, but people who want the nationality to launder money from corruption or financial crime can get it on the spot. this principle is not only allowed but has been promoted in portugal. in 2013 and 2014, the right-wing government in portugal went on a real tour of russia selling portuguese gold visas. in the same months as the euromaidan and with particular intensity after the invasion of crimea, the portuguese government was luring russian millionaires into this very regrettable scheme. and even today, even with a war and a criminal and illegal invasion of ukraine, the portuguese government, now socialist, continues to refuse to revoke these gold visas and to penalize the elite who are supporting and financing this invasion.

in writing. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, more than 2 million refugees. we'll probably be down to three by the end of the week, and if this war doesn't stop, it won't be long before that number doubles. they are overwhelmingly women and children. these are absolutely innocent people who, without any explanation and for no reason, have seen their lives fall apart in front of the eyes of the entire world. i want, however, to leave words of gratitude. countries, governments, peoples are mobilized in a movement of solidarity that should leave us proud and hopeful for a better world. if on other occasions, probably for bad reasons, we have failed to show the solidarity we ought to have shown to people who need us, this time europe and europeans are responding. solidarity alone will not be enough to cope with the largest movement of refugees in europe since the second world war. we're going to need the means, money, housing, schools, to provide health care for these people. we started with solidarity and we did well. let's do the rest now because these people don't have time to waste any more of their lives.
ladies and gentlemen, in february 2020, before the pandemic began, women faced numerous political, social and economic inequalities, some of which have been accentuated in the last two years or at least have become more visible, such as precarious employment and therefore higher unemployment, or the lack of reconciliation between personal, professional and family life. in the european parliament, in all the committees we work in, from foreign policy to the economy, from the budget to the internal market, from agriculture to fisheries, we try to bridge these and other inequalities. i'm not saying that we've done better in this legislature than we have in previous legislatures. but we know that everything is not okay. the commission has already given some figures which show that there is still an imbalance between the representation of men and women. as my colleague robert biedroń has said, when we look at the committees' choices, we are not very happy. for example, in the special committee on artificial intelligence, the final report was attended by only men, both the shadow rapporteurs and the rapporteur. as caroline criado perez wrote in her book "invisible women", the real reason we exclude women is because we see the rights of 50 percent of the population as the interests of a minority.

the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat fraud. madam president, commissioners, i would first like to congratulate my colleagues on the committee on employment and social affairs and the committee on economic and monetary affairs. the committee on budgets underlined that nextgenerationeu is having a stabilising effect on member states and that the recovery and resilience mechanism required further adaptation of the european semester. for the promotion of inclusive sustainable growth all options must be considered, including the revision of the stability and growth pact, the importance of the existence of a european commission capacity to go to financial markets on behalf of the european union. finally, the success of the recovery and resilience mechanism should be the basis for redefining the european semester as a privileged instrument for the coordination of economic, budgetary, social and environmental policies, with renewed democratic legitimacy, greater accountability, and more parliamentary oversight. a european semester with identity, which should be given to it by a regulation of its own based on lessons learned from the governance of the recovery and resilience mechanism.
on behalf of the left group. madam president, the economic rules of the european union are obsolete and are and have always been counterproductive. they were suspended during the pandemic because everyone knew they couldn't afford to respond to this economic crisis anymore. and we know today that they will not allow us to respond to the economic crisis that is coming and will be provoked by the russian invasion of ukraine, either from the point of view of responding to the economic and social crisis, or from the point of view of the investment that is essential in the energy transition so that europe can reduce its dependence on russia in this field. that is why the suspension of economic rules is a window of opportunity to change them, a window of opportunity which this report contributes to squandering. the report calls for a clear path to the revision of the rules, but it does not have a single clear, substantive commitment on how that revision should be done. it has vague ideas and paragraphs that say one thing and its opposite, as we also heard in commissioner dombrovskis' speech. it even has the famous references to the so-called prudent fiscal policies that have destroyed so many economies within the european union. we have shared with the socialists and the greens our common views on the need to review the economic rules of the european union. the strategy of the right is clear, it is to postpone and delay this debate to leave everything the same when this window of opportunity closes. this report plays that game and contributes to the loss of that opportunity.
madam president, the annual growth review is a kick-off to the european semester. this year, if indeed the last of the general derogation clause of the stability and growth pact, is crucial for the debate and above all for the decisions on the future of economic governance in the european union. but let's be clear, we are not and will not grow to the level we expected. economic forecasts for this final stretch of the pandemic are already moving forward with more modest numbers, but the outbreak of war in ukraine is significantly changing our expectations. economic and budgetary policy cannot ignore it. if the revision of budget rules was necessary for recovery from the pandemic, it became urgent in the response to the war. this review must be completed before the end of the year, otherwise we will lose the reformist impetus that has emerged from the challenges the union is now facing. realistic paths to deficit reduction, responsibility in managing national public debts and ambition in issuing joint debt in the union are three pillars of this debate. we must be bold in our solutions, but at the same time be aware that we have a duty of solidarity towards future generations. we want more europe, more unity and more solidarity precisely because we want a better future.

madam president, madam prime minister, dear high representative, colleagues, the war that shocks us with its atrocities is now infiltrating our lives through the economy. it's the cost of fuel for our cars, the meat on our table, the bread. but let us not allow the economic consequences to dictate any concession to barbaric aggression, for that would be a failure of humanism and solidarity which we would never forgive ourselves. we bear the cost of defending freedom. today it's the ukrainians, tomorrow it could be us. as with the pandemic, it is crucial that the eu acts quickly before the war turns into a deep crisis. a major economic plan to respond to the war crisis is therefore indispensable. it will not be the redirection of nextgenerationeu, whose funds are needed for the structural investment deficit of the european union. a new plan with new common resources is needed, a nextgenerationeu 2.0. the success we have had in not lacking ambition to respond to the covid-19 crisis should guide us now to respond further to this challenge, which is challenging the union itself.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the present situation only reinforces the importance of ensuring greater energy independence. this autonomy means greater use of electricity with the corresponding progressive abandonment of fossil fuels, of fossil energies. but to do that, we have to be more self-sufficient not only in electricity generation, but also in the development, promotion, production and recycling of batteries and their components. with planet earth's finite resources, we need to be able to use and reuse these components. this can only be done by a strong commitment to the circular economy and also to the battery side. it is therefore important to recall here the general objective of achieving a significant level of recovery of materials from batteries, which is not being achieved. i would like to reiterate my support for the overall objectives of the proposal: to strengthen the functioning of the internal market, in particular for products, processes and waste, by ensuring a fair market and conditions of competition with common rules and standards, to promote the circular economy of the various components and to reduce the environmental and social impacts of all stages of the battery life cycle. we can, however, go further with our vote by facilitating the process of removing and replacing batteries in light vehicles and a transparent labelling policy for the consumer. big changes are made with small steps, and big wins are made with small breakthroughs. if we want energy independence, decarbonization and moving forward in electrification, moving beyond fossil fuels, then let's move forward in these matters. for those who want to learn, ignorance is merely a step. may we continue to learn and live sustainably within the limits of our planet.
madam president, this eighth environment action programme is an essential instrument to guide the european union in its environmental and climate objectives for the next few years with a view to 2050. our response to climate and biodiversity emergencies requires the implementation of a holistic approach, taking into account mitigation and adaptation measures, the circular economy, the protection and recovery of biodiversity and the zero pollution target. this approach will also have to take account of the specificities of european regions and that they have different starting points. i would like to draw attention to the outermost regions, such as madeira and the azores. it is also essential to include the local level in the decision-making process, which will help to contribute to territorial cohesion in the eu. now more than ever, we have all the signs that we must reduce our dependence on fossil fuels and end our investments in them as quickly as possible. the answer, and the only way to save the planet for future generations, is to transition to a sustainable, welfare economy.
it is a strange thing, mr president, commissioner, that we should be talking about safety at work when in a neighbouring country nothing is safe and civilians are being bombed. but we are talking about the future and the future must be good for workers. it is good that a new framework for health and safety in the workplace is emerging. cancer still causes the most work-related deaths. so we must try to eliminate dangerous substances. digitalisation is of course very efficient, but it is also often a source of stress, especially for older workers. it is important that older workers are given time to adapt to these new techniques. employers will have to take care of that. stress causes a lot of mental problems. it would therefore be good to strike a balance between the work situation and the home situation, because very often you see that particularly older workers have a caring function at home in addition to their work. it would be good if employers took this into account more often. in belgium we have the right to disconnect and there is talk of this too. it would perhaps be a good idea to introduce this at european level. rotation can also be a problem. in this parliament, for example, officials have to rotate every five years. that's actually discriminatory, especially for older employees, because once you have your rhythm and knowledge, you have to go to a new workplace. and then you're supposed to start over as a junior in a new department. that often causes mental stress. we are advocating prevention in the workplace and a healthy workplace. we are calling for employers to invest more in preventive health care, because that is an investment in future savings. we need these workers, now and in the future.
mr president, i would like to thank you. i would like to begin by thanking my danish colleague, marianne vind, for her excellent work. the measures presented in the report are important for protecting workers in europe and for creating a sustainable working life. it is therefore important that we now raise our ambitions in the field of occupational safety and health. the legislation and the strategy for health and safety at work must be based on the reality of the present day, including a dialogue with the social partners, who are after all the closest to the employees. having a job to go to is important, but people should also come home from a safe workplace. we're going to start from the perspective of a sustainable professional life. it is good for the workers, and also for the european labour market. for too many, it is instead marked by insecurity and risk. stronger protection is needed for health and safety risks, as no one should be at risk of accidents or long-term illness. the reality is also that people die going to work, to provide for their families, to put food on the table, to create a better future for themselves and their children. a zero vision is needed to actively prevent work-related accidents and illnesses. cancer accounts for 52% of all work-related deaths in the eu. these are frightening numbers. asbestos has been banned since 2005. yet we are exposed to it daily in our schools, in our workplaces, in our homes. a european strategy for the cleanup of all asbestos will save lives. we have a long way to go, but reflection also increases the pace of progress. together we will ensure a sustainable working life for all workers in europe. together, we'll make sure no one dies on the job.
mr president, accidents at work, occupational diseases and health and safety problems at work are not inseparable from existing working conditions, precariousness, the imposed rhythms of work or the deregulation of working hours. the right to safety, hygiene and health at work is a social right of workers which requires respect for the right to physical integrity, the prevention of accidents, occupational diseases, work-related diseases and the promotion of health at the workplace. in portugal, this right is a constitutional right, and it is the responsibility of employers to take measures to guarantee this right, and it is the responsibility of the state, through labour inspections, to effectively monitor compliance with it and to take punitive action in the event of non-compliance. much remains to be done to reduce work accidents drastically, particularly fatal ones. at the same time, it is essential to increase social protection and effective follow-up measures for returning workers to work. some occupational diseases which, although not classified as such, are in fact truly so. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the social partners are able to take part in the negotiations, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for his report. in the 21st century, the right to safety, hygiene and health at work cannot be a mirage.
mr president, given the high level of health protection we aim to achieve in all union policies and actions, it is essential that we focus on extending vision zero to cover also the active prevention of work-related accidents and diseases, in particular by addressing psychosocial risks and mental health, adapting to digital, environmental and demographic transitions, minimising negative impacts and enhancing the positive effects on health literacy and its role in recognising the risks and modifiable risk factors, in particular those linked to cancer, which still represent the main cause of work-related death. this is a realistic and progressive report calling for better working conditions and safety for workers and it is our duty to ensure that it is consistent.
on behalf of the ppe group. mr president, taxation is at the heart of the political debate. on the one hand, we have the positive impulses of the agreement on the reform of the international tax system and the recent initiatives of the commission, and on the other hand, we have the negative impulses of the tax scandals and the economic crisis resulting from the pandemic. the response to this crisis cannot ignore the tax dimension. we are also beginning to feel the effects of the war that has broken out in europe and we are all feeling the impact of energy taxation in our pockets. here it becomes clear that taxes will play a key role in the recovery of the european economy and in the green and digital transition. this report is therefore timely and i would like to congratulate my colleague and friend mr luděk niedermayer on the proposals he has brought to this parliament. i am not going to go into the details of the proposals. simplify tax processes for citizens and businesses with less red tape and associated costs, reflect on the role of positive tax incentives and balance with free competition in the internal market, focus on digitalisation with electronic invoices, harmonised models and a one-stop shop, improve information exchange systems and enhance european platforms, and mitigate tax distortion in favour of debt. as i said, taxation is on the agenda and european taxpayers want more than statements, they want concrete action. we're here to be and we're here to demand it.
rapporteur. – mr president, in these difficult times we are facing, in which, as the commissioner was just referring to in the previous debate, we will need significant amounts of resources to face the challenges in front of us because of this war. i am pleased that in this report we managed to achieve a broad majority supporting fairer and simpler taxation in the field of withholding taxes, guaranteeing progress in different issues within our discussions. there was a positive environment of discussion between those more actively participating in the negotiations, and even at this late hour in the evening many of the shadow rapporteurs are here with us – i see as isabel, billy, i see ernest, some of our colleagues that were our shadows and probably others are at home, but they were certainly intensely involved in the debate. i want to thank them and want to thank the secretariat, obviously, of the fisc subcommittee, but also our group's team and my own team that helped with all this work and endeavour we did together. the final result, i think, mirrors this positive environment and constructive debate we had. we want to increase the effectiveness of our tax systems, reducing avoidance and fraud risks. we want to improve cross-border investments and lower the administrative burden. these efforts should go and in hand, and this is the tone of this report on the european withholding tax framework. after the many recent scandals regarding tax abuse, the european parliament has the responsibility to address the shortcomings that still allow profit-shifting and aggressive tax planning to continue. the report starts by welcoming the oecd inclusive framework agreement, including the global minimum effective corporate tax for multinational corporations. this is an historic moment, and we call on the council to ensure the agreement comes into effect already in 2023. in light of this positive momentum created, we expect the commission to present a legislative proposal for an eu-wide withholding tax in order to ensure that payments generated within the union are taxed at least once before leaving it. this is particularly important in the current environment we are living in. we cannot allow financial resources to leave the union without being subject to taxation while facing inflationary pressure due to the severe shocks in the supply of energy, of essential goods, all the consequences of what we are facing at these moments. the report calls on the commission and the member states to ensure that all payments, including dividends, royalties and interest generated in the eu are taxed at an effective rate. these payments should cease to be a means of profit-shifting. the review of the ird and psd directives should be on the table and then in light of the ‘cum-ex’ and cum—cum’ scandals, it is clear that further cooperation and assistance between tax authorities is needed. this fraud was responsible for eur 140 billion lost in revenues in just 11 member states. therefore we must act swiftly and decisively. therefore we call on the commission to extend the mandatory exchange of information to dividend arbitrage schemes and capital gains, including granting refunds, but also to assess the extension of reporting requirements to cross-border arrangements. the final chapter of the report is focused on simpler taxation and removing barriers to investments in the single market, building on the commitment to complete the capital markets union, which is also something very important for the completion of our monetary union. repayments of withholding taxes remain predominantly a paper-driven process: slow, burdensome and more prone to fraud. in the past, the commission issued recommendations about simplification, but the results, unfortunately, are not totally convincing. with this in mind, we welcome the intention of the commission to pursue legislative harmonisation for a common and standardised system of withholding taxes. there is more than one way to go in this direction, as it was exposed in the impact assessment. for the moment, pursuing legal harmonisation of procedures is the most sensible and feasible path. this can be complemented with the use of digital technologies, making the resulting system more efficient in the countries, but also to facilitate seamless procedures between different national systems. a full relief-at-source system should only be considered at a later stage and cannot be in any way detrimental to the fight against tax abuse. colleagues i think the negotiation team managed to find a very positive and balanced text that delivers a win-win approach. again, i thank you for your support in those negotiations, and i certainly call on your support in the vote tomorrow on this important report about fairer and simpler taxation in the eu.
mr president, the tax scandals, of which the 'pandora papers' are the most pressing and recent example, are leading our citizens, well, to demand more from policy-makers. at international level, the agreement reached at oecd level on the reform of the international tax system is a paradigmatic one. we in the union now have a responsibility to take the lead in implementing it. at european level, we are committed to making the new framework for taxation of business income, befit, an instrument for the sake of fairness and tax transparency, but also for the competitiveness of our companies. national withholding systems thus emerge as one of the areas where we can and must go further, improving cooperation between the 27 and safeguarding the integrity of our internal market. on the one hand, withholding tax ensures recurring revenues for national budgets, helps to combat profit shifting and helps to prevent tax evasion and avoidance. on the other hand, we face risks of double taxation, of deterrence of cross-border investment and of bureaucratic overload for taxpayers. it is to enhance the benefits of these systems and to mitigate their risks that we want a clearer european framework. this european framework requires the unlocking of long-standing european legislation such as the interest and royalties directive, the approximation of the rules on reduction and exemption and the improvement of information exchange systems. the fight against fraud and evasion and the commitment to fairer, more transparent and competitive tax systems have several fronts. this is one of them. and here we are ready to take over.
rapporteur. - mr president, commissioner, thank you for your words and especially for the colleagues who have spoken. i would say that perhaps the most important thing is the broad unity we have shown in our priorities, which go beyond the equally important simplification of the taxation and withholding system. we demand, we ask, we agree on measures which are precisely linked to this common system of withholding tax at european level and which prevent the outflow of profits, these gigantic profits, from the european union to tax havens, as the examples given here by the chairman of the fisc subcommittee, paul tang. now that we have a global agreement on corporate taxation, we also need to move in a coordinated way in this direction. as i said, commissioner, beyond simplification, we demand and ask the commission for courage in the proposals that come in and we will certainly demand from the members of the council that they be consistent at this time when we will need resources for a just response to the crisis that we have ahead of us.
madam president, russia's military aggression against ukraine has shown how important it is to overcome europe's dependence on russia. an economic dependence which has become also a political dependence in response to this military aggression. it is important that this energy sovereignty of europe be achieved with an energy transition agenda, without hesitation and zigzagging, and not through a return to the coal age. and it is also important that the truly solidarity-based and responsible response that is being given to the refugee crisis of this war in ukraine extends to all refugees of all backgrounds, colours and religions. europe cannot have two asylum policies, it cannot discriminate between human lives. but it is also important that the victims of this war are not used to remilitarize europe, by enriching the same european military industry which has sold some of the weapons with which russia is now attacking the ukrainian people. ten european countries continued to sell arms to russia even after the embargo imposed by the european institutions. remiliarization is the promotion of this industry, which will then spread weapons around the world. and it is also, and it cannot be, the whitewashing and strengthening of nato, an international organization which is responsible for so many criminal wars, for so many millions of dead and refugees across this planet. the path of peace is the path of an autonomous diplomacy of the european union, a diplomacy for peace.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the cruel and unjustified invasion of ukraine is causing a real humanitarian disaster: ten million displaced people, many of them to the european union. the solidarity with those we receive must be matched by the means we use to welcome and integrate them. the response to war is not just sanctions or military support for ukraine. it must also be given the means to support refugees here in europe and to deal with the social consequences of this war. it is the most disadvantaged people who suffer the most from the impact of escalating prices, energy or food. we cannot allow the increase in poverty, particularly energy poverty, to be the price to pay for this war. we have been able to create the next generation eu to respond to the covid crisis. let us also know how to innovate, let us know how to rise to the occasion in this struggle which is also our struggle for freedom and democracy.
madam president, what is expected from this european council is a european response to support ukraine and ukrainians, those who remain in the country and those who join the european union. that it punishes the invader without cutting off the russian people who, like us, oppose this invasion. that it takes on new priorities, like defense and energy security. the commission will continue to monitor the implementation of the union's macro-financial assistance to the countries of central and eastern europe, including the countries of the former soviet union, in order to ensure that the union's macro-financial assistance is consistent with the union's macro-financial assistance. add, not replace. protecting european citizens from security, but also from the impact of sanctions on european economies. they cannot be the most vulnerable to pay for the rises in energy or food prices. it paves the way for a strategic autonomy of the european union in all its dimensions, including energy. a robust european response is expected, as was the european and robust response to the pandemic crisis.
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, it should not be for the sake of this absurd war, but for the sake of justice for farmers, who are men and women who want to produce in a way that respects good environmental practices. but the truth is that agriculture, food security and food sovereignty have come to the political agenda for these reasons that shouldn't exist. the reason is that we can now face reality and stop the ideology and ideological fighting that makes no sense. the epp must of course defend european farmers. we have to produce more because europe has to feed more. and, madam president, commissioner, exceptional situations call for exceptional measures. i therefore propose that the commission should review the objectives and timetables of the prairie to prairie and biodiversity strategies. reviewing the deadlines, not ending them. the legislative process should be suspended. and finally, commissioner, increase, but make it urgent, the support for farmers.
as a result of the invasion of ukraine by the russian federation, the european union is now in the early stages of a food crisis, triggered by disruption in supply and distribution chains and by the exponential rise in fuel prices. a detailed action plan is needed to ensure the eu's strategic autonomy, including the autonomy and resilience of the food system. to do this, our farming and fishing systems must become more sustainable and future-oriented, with the aim of reducing dependence on imports and increasing domestic production. we must reduce our dependence on fossil fuels and fertilisers by looking for alternatives, but always guaranteeing the protection of our farmers, in particular through the common agricultural policy. in this regard, we should take advantage of the measures already planned and adopted at european level such as the european green deal and the eu biodiversity and prairie to prairie strategies which will contribute to mitigating this crisis and reducing uncertainty in the affected sectors. in resolving this crisis, we must maintain food trade flows in all european regions, particularly those which, because of their distance from the continental shelf, may be disproportionately affected, such as the outermost regions.
the support for ukraine and the eu's response capacity to deal with the consequences of the war must be translated into concrete action. we are faced with humanitarian tragedies, while we are already beginning to feel the economic consequences of this inexplicable war. this reality is already reflected in the prices we pay for food products, which, in addition to speculation, are reflected in the turbulence of supply chains, the price of fuel and the transport of food, and reflect the lack of such fundamental materials as fertilisers. i therefore join those who argue that the eu civil protection mechanism should be activated. the humanitarian aspect, no doubt, but also to try to prevent risks to the food security of the eu that we can foresee, which could result from an escalation or prolongation of this war. the eu is therefore advised to have an action plan capable of securing supply chains by using 'food corridors' by sea, rail or road, while accelerating measures with an impact on the agricultural sector.
member of the commission. – madam president, thank you very much for this debate, in particular for the report and for your extremely valuable and supportive contribution. we really count on this if we will make our proposals for the modification, the revision of the financial regulation. indeed i think it’s important to make progress, i cannot agree more with what you have said. i think what we need is also your support in your member states, because there are some governments which hide behind the data protection issues, but in reality it’s because there are weaknesses of their it systems which prevent them from engaging in interoperability and so on. by the way, next generation eu now offers a lot of possibilities to invest, in particular in digitalisation. some member states consider this again as a kind of transfer of another kind of sovereignty to brussels or whatsoever. this is all nonsense. it’s really about taking care about our union budget and really identifying at a very early stage if there is a misuse, as mr fernandes said, very often the final beneficiaries are making errors because we have to admit that our regulations are not the simplest. so, it’s always a mixture between the fraudulent issues and simply errors, and i think with an it system in place, we can avoid a lot of problems, which is finally in the interests of all of us, and would make also our life in terms of administrative procedures much easier. once again, thank you for this debate and i wish you all a good rest.
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the following areas: the commission has already done so. i am not saying that we should not be concerned about the future of the community. thank you very much for that. oligarchic structures are a structural problem. this cannot be resolved by any public prosecution of individuals, but by changing the structures. the commission has also proposed that the european parliament should be consulted on the draft budget. the transparency in monitoring where european union funds are going is unfortunately not fully achieved to date, simply because of national resistance and resistance by the member states. we often have to rely on national authorities, and if the national authorities do not function properly or if there is historical corruption, we are not able to identify these structures early and quickly enough to prevent, for example, excessive concentration and preference, interruptions, for example in tenders, the recurrence of the same ones, which can then be enriched accordingly, that tenders are undermined, that there is systematic fraud, for example. we actually need to do better. but i would also like to say to those who have just spoken on the left that it would also help to tell the truth. we must be clear: the arrest of boyko borisov was not in accordance with the rule of law. it was carried out without a judge, it was carried out without prosecutors and then imagine that in his time bojko borisov had simply had the police arrest the socialist opposition leader. but if something had happened, you would have stood there and yelled at me. if you want to maintain the rule of law and fight corruption, you have to follow the rules and laws yourself period. and no matter who is accused, the citizen and the head of government are the same: we need the evidence, we need investigations, we need the support for the european public prosecutor's office to do its job. and these arrests - in writing. it is regrettable that, even in the midst of the war in ukraine, the european parliament is launching a political attack against member states which have made the greatest contribution to the eu's solidarity with those fleeing the war, threatening to withhold funds from them. the european parliament's report is another brutal political attack and baseless accusation, based on tabloid rumors and fake news, with clear political aims. while objective assessments show that hungary and poland are increasingly effective in tackling the misuse of eu funds and corruption, the commission has also noted that the eu is not yet fully aware of the risks of corruption in the eu. commissioner hahn listed the control systems available, the various financial instruments to ensure that the aid is used correctly and said that both the eu and the member states have zero tolerance for irregularities and abuses and that the commission has a duty to ensure that the aid is used correctly. these tools and systems need to be used more effectively, rather than political attacks on member states and the disintegration of eu unity. this is the thinking that led to the arrest of former bulgarian prime minister boyko borisov for one night after a visit by the european prosecutor general, reportedly without a judicial mandate and proper suspicion, and then to the fact that meps from the european people's party travelled to bulgaria to conduct a fact-finding inquiry into the incident. this approach is incompatible with the rule of law and the principle of subsidiarity. i took it.
in writing. the protection of european union funds against fraud is of paramount importance, especially in the context of the financial crisis we have been facing for several years. eu funds must be used in strict compliance with the principles of sound financial management and subject to full and unlimited control by law at eu level, including the ep. this will increase transparency and responsiveness to citizens' needs and ensure that taxpayers' money is spent properly. scandals involving fraud, conflicts of interest, corruption and other illegal activities affecting the eu's financial interests involving high-level politicians in the member states and the eu institutions, as well as customs, cross-border and digital fraud and crime, often taking over the structures and support of criminal organisations, are regularly reported by the media across europe. we need to complete this procedure as soon as possible. the financial management of eu funds is of paramount importance. it is unacceptable that the databases of beneficiaries of eu funds do not contain information on final beneficiaries and actual beneficiaries. such actions lead to abuses and the commission must strengthen its capacity to prevent and detect fraud.
