madam president, we're going to end our dependence on russia! let's summarize the facts first. the aggressor in an unprecedented conflict that is taking place just a few hundred kilometres from the borders of the european union is russia. the country that was attacked is ukraine, but it has defended itself heroically, and i want to pay tribute to it from this place, and to the courage of the people there. and also to thank all those who support and help the ukrainian defense in any way. and of course, they also help war refugees. but above all, on the floor of the european parliament, i feel the need to appeal for an end to dependence - especially energy dependence - on the aggressor, which is putin's evil empire. let's say it out loud! if we do not take the necessary steps, we will indirectly continue to encourage the imperial russian policy and sooner or later we will undoubtedly see more explosions. so i repeat, no more dependence on the aggressor! in the name of the victims of this war, to whom we bid a painful farewell and who, i trust, will not be forgotten. and with the knowledge that europe has made a significant and, indeed, a much-needed sacrifice. the victim is her naivete, the face of russian imperialism.
madam president, together in this house we are called ‘the european union’. we are safe and our citizens feel that they have a future. the union can continue to develop, despite sometimes being hesitant. however, decision makers have ignored what might happen and did not engage enough in investing in modernising the common project. they locked the doors and the windows to our house, hoping no one would ever get in. but now our neighbourhood is on fire and the flames might reach our house soon. they are in our backyard and, together with our strategic partner, the united states, we need to put out the fire by moving forward at a faster pace, meaning we need to: consolidate our defence in partnership with nato; protect our population against energy and food crises; financially support ukraine, eu border countries and partners like the republic of moldova; make european funding more flexible; reform the eu. i call on all the international leaders present in brussels to consolidate this house, defend our values by investing in education, our democracy and culture, and listen to our citizens. keep the door open and be courageous enough to protect our neighbourhood.
madam president, one year ago, together with other meps, i proposed a joint motion for a resolution on the 10th anniversary of the uprising in syria. in it we called on the commission to prepare an action plan on impunity to include a specific chapter on syria. unfortunately, so far nothing has been done, despite the request being the central plank of a resolution that enjoyed cross-party support. the eu has been at the forefront of the fight against impunity, providing technical and financial assistance for accountability efforts. however, the invasion of ukraine should now provide the motivation for the european commission to proceed with an action plan. russia’s behaviour in ukraine is the brutal standard of warfare, in particular siege tactics and targeting of civilian infrastructure that is such a feature of the russian involvement in syria. the eu has to get tough on impunity to protect the staff involved in the humanitarian relief efforts in ukraine, in syria and elsewhere.
madam president, the invasion of ukraine has naturally generated great instability on the markets and a sharp rise in fuel prices, and this increase has naturally affected several sectors, including the fisheries sector. but we know that there are large tax exemptions for fossil fuels in fisheries and also subsidies to this industry, and it is not in this way that we can help small-scale fishing and diversify activities within a blue economy. and so, considering that the fisheries sector does not pay around €1.5 billion a year in taxes - and therefore is not collected by the member states - we need to rethink how to help this sector in the transition to a truly green economy. and that is why we must use all the funds at our disposal, in particular the fisheries fund, to diversify the opportunity for fishermen, and in particular small-scale fishermen, to modernise their fleet, to have cleaner engines, engines that are really more decarbonised, and to be able to adapt to new professions within a blue economy. without this, we will not be able to make the transition to a decarbonised economy in the fisheries sector.
madam president, despite the rain in recent days, much of the portuguese territory remains in a state of severe or extreme drought. with the sanctions under the pretext of war and criminal speculation, the escalation of prices and the shortage of raw materials are further suffocating small and mediumsized farms. it is therefore necessary to support farmers and to mitigate the increase in production costs, starting immediately with the fight against the speculative rise in the prices of fuel, electricity, fertilisers and feedstuffs, by confronting the monopolies and the large-scale distribution which crush the prices of national production. it is also time to question the cap policies and its model of over-intensive farming and monoculture, which is a drain on the water we do not have. the water we have should be used for the food we need as bread for our mouths, for the promotion of our food sovereignty and our right to produce. we welcome the farmers who will be taking to the streets in braga tomorrow in defence of national production and their work.
in writing. the political impotence and short-sightedness of so-called western leaders have brought europe to a state of dependence that is on the verge of destroying the european economy. the imposition of the 'green deal' led to a huge jump in electricity prices. it has to be said that despite the billions poured into 'green energy', fossil fuels dominate the overall energy mix. just to give you an example, in germany, which is a leader in green transition, it looks like this: 77% of the country's primary energy comes from oil, gas and coal, and only 5.2% comes from solar and wind. despite the facts, the mad push for a green utopia continues, with member states being pressured to close their only domestically-generated power plants - the chp plants. in bulgaria, this is most striking for people working in the maritsa basin, which provides a livelihood for over 20,000 people. if the eu wants affordable and sustainable energy, it must stop the madness of the 'green deal'.
in writing. the war that has been going on for a month has caused inestimable humanitarian losses. it also contributed to the deepening and prolonging of the energy crisis affecting europe. in this critical situation, any suggestion or idea has a lot of weight and therefore pays particular attention to the european commission's repowereu initiative. we must protect all european citizens from the effects of soaring energy prices. it is unacceptable that the people pay the price of war. i therefore welcome the fact that the commission has finally recognised that the most effective way to achieve this objective is through an official price regime following the pattern of the hungarian reduction in resizes. instead of left-wing fanatics, especially now, we need stable, well-functioning measures. in addition to protecting civilians, strategic calm and foresight are needed now. i therefore consider the commission's proposal on the mandatory minimum level of filling of natural gas storage tanks to be considered. we need to avoid the energy price explosion we experienced in december and january, and we need to start preparing for the next winter now. finally, we must not allow irresponsible proposals that would jeopardise europe's energy supply and bring the continent to the brink of war. it's risky, we have to stay out of this war. we need to keep our societies and economies running, and that requires all reliable european energy sources, including nuclear energy.
in writing. in the face of russia's brutal aggression against ukraine, energy independence and security of supply must be one of our top priorities. i have no doubt that cutting off russia's revenues from the gas, oil and coal that fund its war machine is a matter of the utmost importance. however, we must also balance the consequences of these actions, which have an impact on high energy prices and pose a real threat to the functioning of european businesses and contribute to energy poverty in households. we must therefore use all the means at our disposal to make the most of all possible sources of energy, to become completely independent of russian supplies as soon as possible. in this context, i welcomed the proposal for the repowereu programme, which not only pledges a significant reduction in imports from russia by the end of this year, but also proposes a solution to the problem of rising energy prices for consumers and obliges member states to replenish gas stocks before the coming winter.
written. the russian invasion of ukraine marks a new chapter in the history of europe. it is clear that security changes in our immediate environment dictate the reform of our energy policy. in my view, diversifying supply routes and focusing on renewable energy sources must help us to ensure two main objectives: (1) security of supply and (2) stable, affordable and competitive prices for both consumers and european businesses. for too long, the profits have been privatized, while the taxpayers have borne the risks. this crisis offers us an opportunity to move towards a fairer energy union in which the right to clean and affordable energy is guaranteed to every european citizen. replacing two-thirds of russia's imported gas in less than 10 months will not be a small task. increasing lng imports will result in significant costs for all energy consumers, so we must be careful not to replace one dependency with a new and much more expensive one. the success of the plan will ultimately depend on two factors: on the one hand, the political will to mobilise significant investments in interconnections, renewable energy sources and clean technologies, and on the other hand, the necessary regulatory decisions to prepare well for the next winter to be taken in a very short time.
in writing. europe has had a naive energy policy for years. the rashness of the actions and the deepening of the dependence on the supply of raw materials from russia was an unforgivable strategic mistake. the long-term energy security of our continent has been neglected. the nord stream 2 project is a prime example of this approach, which was pushed through despite the legitimate objections of many member states. i'm glad we're finally making a decisive change of direction and moving on. i only regret that it was only after russia invaded ukraine that some people were able to see things clearly. dependence on russian energy supplies is now a top priority. in the short term, to increase economic pressure on russia, and in the long term, to ensure europe's energy security. joint action cannot be limited to focusing on renewable energy the need for a rational, realistic and multi-dimensional approach. we need to combine the development of renewable energy with the development of infrastructure that will allow supplies from other countries (i would like to point out here, for example, the baltic gas pipeline) and with a strategic approach to our own extraction of raw materials. supporting citizens and businesses is also absolutely fundamental. the war on the eastern border of the european union has already affected energy prices, and it's only been a month since the day of the invasion. if we don't take the right steps to diversify our supply of raw materials, stockpile, strengthen our energy independence and limit the rise in retail prices, things will look very bad before the next winter.
in writing. following the russian invasion of ukraine, the european union has worked to address russia's high energy dependence and the associated alarming price increases, as well as to ensure energy security and supply. the socialists in parliament argue for the need for solutions to tackle rising energy prices, and in particular electricity prices. the union must act now, in a united and coordinated way, to curb the price escalation that is weighing down the economy of citizens and businesses. these solutions include accelerating the deployment of indigenous renewable energies and short-term measures to decouple the price of gas from electricity. at the same time, we must diversify our energy supplies and eliminate our energy dependence on russia. in this respect, spain can play a very important role in diversification, as it has a large lng (liquified natural gas) infrastructure, including around 30% of the union's regasification capacity. but to take advantage of these existing energy infrastructures, the union needs to increase energy interconnections between the iberian peninsula and france.
in writing. member states must actively develop their energy supplies and networks, and russia must be brought to zero, since the eu's security depends heavily on it. it is important to develop alternative energy sources and to increase financing for renewable energy. it is also unacceptable to start paying for gas in russian rubles. we have to find gas reserves for estonia again next year, hopefully with the finnish and swedish partners.
the european union guidelines, implemented by governments that have liberalized and privatised the energy sector, imply the maintenance of oligopolistic markets in which electricity prices are cartelled and where the methodologies adopted guarantee outrageous profits, with huge differences between production costs and consumer prices. the creation and operation of a carbon market with derived products to be traded on a speculative exchange contributes to the worsening of prices and the european union has responsibilities in the current situation. the measures previously proposed by the european commission are based solely on subsidising consumers, i.e. indirectly on the profits that the oligopolies in the sector continue to reap. we have to question profits, rents and margins to ensure that prices are kept down. concrete measures are needed to ensure that consumer prices reflect production costs, and to tackle once and for all the colossal profits that the operation of a highly oligopolistic market allows. and it is imperative that the liberalisation of the sector be reversed by ensuring public control. it's reality that demands it!
madam president, the sixth assessment report of the united nations intergovernmental panel on climate change shows the world how the average temperature of the planet has already risen by 1.1 degrees and is affecting natural and human systems in europe. since the last assessment, the impacts of climate change have increased, resulting in losses in agricultural production, infrastructure or water scarcity. but what it brings back does not soothe, but rather causes anxiety and worry. europe is warming faster than the rest of the planet and will continue to do so, and southern europe will be hit hardest. the fit for 55 package must be implemented and look at the issue of adaptation as central from now on. i cannot, however, address this house without mentioning the russian invasion and barbarism in ukraine, which have, in addition to all the human suffering, caused additional problems of two different orders: food shortages and economic losses, the channelling of resources to the military effort and the blocking of diplomatic advances. ladies and gentlemen, this is not the time to look back before we leave behind any doubts or indecisions. what i'm asking you is that we don't go down in history for lack of courage.
madam president, the latest ipcc report confirms the climate emergency we're going through. it also confirms that without dramatic emission reductions, we will quickly reach tipping points. it also confirms the inadequacy of our current mitigation measures and that adaptation measures are needed to address the risks and harms already caused by climate change. proof of this was the intense drought we experienced in portugal earlier this year, which affected our agriculture, biodiversity and water supply. so we need more. we need to step up our climate efforts. we need more ambition, especially in the fit for 55 legislative package. and we also need more funding. because we all have a right to a sustainable environment and we must fight for it. protecting our own and future generations must be part of this plan.
on behalf of the left group. madam president, the left will obviously support whatever sense of urgency there is, and that exists with the citizens, to put an end to the obscene reality of having some of the largest and most profitable companies in the world paying little or no tax. but what we are promised is a tax on multinational corporations that will be fair, fairly distributed and effective. and the proposal we have on the table is none of those things. it will not be a tax on multinationals, but a tax on a small fraction of multinational companies. it won't be effective because it's full of exclusions, deductions and exceptions. it will not be fair, because it is a much lower rate than that which is practised in most countries and for most companies. and it will not be fairly distributed, because the developed world has tried to keep for itself the lion's share of the revenue from this new tax. we therefore have a lot of urgency, but also a lot to improve on this proposal.
the report on the future of fisheries in the english channel, the north sea, the irish sea and the atlantic ocean in the light of the withdrawal of the united kingdom from the european union was unanimously adopted by the committee on fisheries, a consensus which underlines the importance of the subject for the whole of the union's fishing industry. i am grateful for the cooperation of all the shadow rapporteurs: francisco millán mon, jan huitema, caroline roose, france jamet, bert-jan ruissen and joão pimenta lopes. this is the first time that the european parliament has looked at how the trade and cooperation agreement, which was signed between the union and the united kingdom, is being implemented, in one of the sectors that has been most affected. the agreement set out a set of conditions and rules for both parties. however, it is clear that some issues relating to fisheries, in particular access for eu vessels to british waters, have not been fully clarified. this has led to various interpretations. the uk has taken advantage of the ambiguity of certain provisions of the agreement to make interpretations which i consider to be abusive. the result is protectionist measures that limit fishing licences for eu vessels, creating unrest in fishing communities, creating uncertainty, causing serious damage and fostering a climate of conflict that benefits no one and should be prevented. we need an attitude of cooperation and loyalty from all sides. we therefore call on the united kingdom to refrain from protectionist action and to promote a better neighbourhood in order to ensure stable and predictable conditions for fishermen. we urge the european commission to consider all options to guarantee the rights of union fishermen, including limiting access to union ports for uk vessels or even restricting the import of fishery products, measures which, while considered extreme, should not be dismissed as they may be, at best, a necessary tool for the protection of european fishermen. we believe that the european commission should work with all coastal states on appropriate solutions for better control and the adoption of fisheries management measures in accordance with the common fisheries policy and the european ecological pact. the brutal invasion of ukraine by russia makes it all the more necessary for all the states of europe to understand each other, whether they belong to the union or not. this has been possible in several strategic areas and should also be the case in fisheries. fisheries resources are a common good and fishery products are essential for our food security. the report sets out four guidelines for finding solutions to the problems caused by brexit. first, relations and the future of fisheries in this area will have to be based on good international governance of the oceans and on good neighbourly, peaceful and cooperative relations with all concerned. secondly, the use of this area should be based on fisheries management principles and long-term environmental, social and economic sustainability, based on the best scientific knowledge. the commission has already established that the aid granted to the fisheries sector is compatible with the internal market. they should be extended and the period of application should at least coincide with the transitional period established: 30 june 2026. fourthly, the specialised committee on fisheries, which monitors the implementation of the agreement in the various sectors, should be fully operational and react in a timely manner to the situations identified. for all these reasons, and because the fisheries sector needs it, i hope that this report will be adopted very strongly by the european parliament.
the commission's proposal is to be submitted to the council for adoption. the union fisheries sector has courageously remained in business during the most difficult periods of the covid-19 pandemic. it is now facing at the same time the consequences of brexit, with the scale that has become clear here and the impact of the brutal increase in fuel. it is a sector which must deserve the solidarity and support of the union. it is imperative that this sector feels and knows that it has not been left to its own devices. following this debate, i would like to stress that it is crucial - i am of course pleased, commissioner, that the specialised committee on fisheries is now operational - but it is also crucial that the european parliament is involved in this activity by having observers from the committee on fisheries present at its meetings. this transparency effort will have enormous benefits in terms of effectiveness and involvement of the whole sector in the union. i would also like to stress a theme that has been raised here: the importance of relations with other third countries, in particular norway, iceland and the faroe islands, which, under the pretext of brexit, have been taking unilateral decisions that are putting ancestral balances in question. one example is the loss of the cod quota in svalbard, which has had a significant impact on several member states and particularly on my country, portugal, where cod is the main national dish, as some of you will know. i also want to stress that the issue of the brexit adjustment reserve has to be discussed again. the funds are not sufficient for fisheries and do not provide for compensation until the end of the transitional period and i would like to have clarification from the commission on this matter. in all this brexit and its collateral disruptions, the commission has the solidarity of the european parliament. of course we must go along the path of dialogue, cooperation, concertation and peace, but at the same time parliament calls on the european commission to be vigilant and to respond in a proportionate and appropriate way to the problems that are being identified. this is essential for the sector and we have seen with great concern that this is not always the case on the uk side. we hope that the recommendations of this report of the european parliament, which i have no doubt will be adopted by a large majority, corresponding to the unanimity it has achieved in the committee on fisheries, will be accepted by the european commission. the european fishermen, their hard work and indomitable work deserve this.
mr president, i am here to talk about the outermost regions, which represent 80% of the potential of biodiversity in the european union. to tackle climate and biodiversity emergencies, it is imperative that we take a holistic approach that values and protects our natural resources. protecting and restoring our ecosystems will benefit our planet, but also, in particular, certain sectors, namely agriculture, fisheries, forestry and others. i am therefore disappointed to see that the proposed nature recovery bill, which was due to be tabled at the end of march, has been delayed. i call for this proposal to be presented as soon as possible with ambitious targets and with particular regard to the specificities of the outermost regions. only then will we ensure sustainable development and a decent future for all generations.
mr president, the rising cost of living that workers and the people are feeling in an alarming way is one of the biggest problems facing my country. if, on the one hand, firm and urgent measures are needed to curb the rise in prices of essential goods, such as foodstuffs, on the other hand, it is important to value wages so that workers do not pay the bill of inflation with their own wages. and in this context it is particularly important to increase the productive capacity of each country to ensure the supply of food, reducing external dependence, avoiding food shortages and curbing speculative price increases. for example, it is necessary to promote cereal production and improve the functioning of the food chain in order to ensure fair prices for production. only by increasing the capacity to produce the food that the population needs, to store and to access food commodities, can we combat the enormous imbalances in our food balance. that's what we're committed to.
on behalf of the left group. mr president, we have argued that the development of energy infrastructure should be in line with national energy plans and should contribute to the existence of a public service of quality energy supply at fair prices and accessible to all. the present situation is characterized by the escalation of sanctions which are harmful to all peoples and by speculative prices which weigh heavily on the pockets of citizens and small and mediumsized enterprises. the so-called temporary derogation granted to portugal and spain leaves untouched aspects such as the liberalisation, privatisation and segmentation of the sector or the pricing methodology in wholesale markets which is responsible for outrageous differentials between production costs and consumer prices. even the timid measures of market regulation which they now dare to contemplate could and should have been taken long ago. public and democratic control of the energy sector is the only way to ensure an economically, socially and environmentally sustainable sector, ensuring stability and security of supply.
member of the commission. – madam president, first, i would like to thank nicolae ștefănuță for his draft guidelines for the 2023 budget. the 2023 budget will be the third of the current multiannual financial framework (mff) but it will be a budget to be negotiated and adopted in a completely different geopolitical situation, and he has referred with a lot of examples to this. we are more than a month into the russian war against ukraine. what we see in the news every day is a human tragedy beyond any description. the direct consequences are large and we see unprecedented streams of refugees seeking safety in the union and the potential economic downturn on the global scale. this will inevitably influence our work, including the implementation of the current budget and preparation of next year’s budget. high inflation and persistent uncertainty and risk mean that we all must remain vigilant and cautious. next year’s budget comes at a very challenging time for the union. the russian military aggression in ukraine represents a massive challenge and its evolution is impossible to predict. we cannot commit all our budgetary resources at this stage. we need to keep flexibilities in reserve to continue to react to changing circumstances. at the same time, we must ensure adequate resources for existing priorities, to continue work on long-term projects, notably the recovery from the pandemic and the green and digital transitions. we need to see how we can further accelerate these priorities. against this background, we should collectively see to what extent we can concentrate on a limited number of issues where there can be a real impact. this and next year, more than ever, we need to avoid diluting our budgetary impact by pursuing limited reinforcements across many budget lines. i can assure you that the commission will try to focus the draft budget on the most effective and efficient use of our definitely limited resources. inflation is currently very high and it’s an open question how quickly it will return to more normal levels. this will affect overall salary related expenditure, other indexed expenditure, such as rent, as well as energy bills, both in 2022 and also next year. this will have a major impact on heading 7, where resources are already quite limited. i would therefore urge all institutions to exercise restraint and where new challenges emerge, like on cybersecurity, to look at possibilities for further interinstitutional cooperation. let me now turn to our next steps. in the first trilogue last week, we agreed on the calendar for the budgetary procedure for the financial year 2023. the commission aims to adopt the commission’s statement of estimates at the beginning of june and deliver the full draft budget in all language versions at the beginning of july. today’s debate should increase our mutual understanding and facilitate progress towards a common approach. in this difficult and unprecedented geopolitical situation we need to maintain our flexibility to respond to new developments. our three institutions succeeded over the past years in agreeing on many groundbreaking files that have changed the union’s budgetary and financial landscape. so i trust that with cooperation, dialogue and a real ability to compromise, and mutual understanding we will reach a timely agreement on next year’s budget.
draft opinion of the consultative committee on industry, research and energy. the commission has already made a number of proposals. the itre committee is concerned. that is the nature of those who are responsible for budgets in committees. but the research framework programme - that is our fear - is increasingly becoming a stone-cold of ambition for everything. i believe that the importance of the research framework programme goes far beyond the subject of research and innovation. we need technology, we need innovation to cope with the transformative power of the energy transition, to maintain competitiveness in europe. and more and more new projects - be it the microchip initiative (welcome) or the satellite issue - are becoming important: the issues of energy transition. but these are all topics that are not funded. and our concern is that we continue to be a stone's throw from ambition. and i want to make it clear once again: we will insist that we cannot agree to such projects without a commitment to unblocking and without a commitment to upfrontloading or frontloading. we cannot make a trade-off between what we have decided and the ambitions of our future. either we have more money, or we can't have these ambitions.
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on transport and tourism. madam president, we cannot talk about the eu budget without the reconstruction of ukraine. the effects of war are not confined to borders. they affect us all. after 40 days of massacres and destruction in bucha, irpin, mariupol, i hope we can all agree on one thing: those who committed them must pay! that's why i proposed the creation of the fund for the reconstruction of ukraine financed from the assets of the putin regime and the oligarchs frozen in all member states. please support this fund. i ask you to urgently adopt the measures necessary for our total energy independence, for food autonomy and for the security of our citizens. as we also strengthen the union's objectives on the environment, transport infrastructure, health and education, we strengthen justice and the rule of law in all member states. the budget of the european union should contain additional support for the republic of moldova, romania and all countries receiving and integrating european citizens on their territory.
the committee on culture and education has tabled an opinion. the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments. culture is a national competence and therefore it is difficult to discuss cultural budgets in an eu context. but with the project of the new european bauhaus we have a unique opportunity to use culture to support one of the most important common objectives, one of the most important common european struggles, namely the fight for the climate and the environment. the bauhaus can help make the green transition tangible and attractive to the citizens of europe. this includes architecture, design and green urban development. we can show eu citizens in their everyday lives how the community is fighting climate in an aesthetic, inclusive, sustainable and european way. with culture as a kind of can opener, we are going to create a cultural narrative about one of the most important political issues of our time. but if we are to succeed, the eu must invest in the bauhaus project. and we need more money to get a dedicated project, so this is my request at this stage: let's bet on the bauhaus.
rapporteur for the opinion of the committee on women’s rights and gender equality. – madam president, dear mr commissioner, dear mr rapporteur, dear colleagues, on behalf of the committee on women’s rights and gender equality, i would like to thank you very much for the constructive cooperation on the guidelines for the 2023 budget, section iii. gender equality is a core value of the european union with significant potential for the eu budget that is still not fully used, as even the european court of auditors concluded in its special report recently. the femm committee therefore adopted a comprehensive opinion with several suggestions for the parliament’s guidelines. among them, a strong commitment to gender mainstreaming and gender budgeting in all stages of the budget cycle; the systematic collection, reporting and evaluation of gender—disaggregated data to accompany a new commission methodology to measure gender—relevant expenditure; a call for significant funding for the daphne programme, in particular in the context of the pandemic and gender—based violence; and last but not least, forced investments in public services, care and gender—responsive cohesion policy. besides the general rapporteur, i would also like to thank all of my colleagues in the budget committee that worked on strengthening the text from a gender—equality perspective. i am very much looking forward to our continued cooperation to make the 2023 budget a significant budget from the perspective of gender equality.
on behalf of the ppe group. - madam president! - i have to go. i'm not going to let you do this. we're closing the first phase of the 2023 budget today. it's never business as usual, there are always some extraordinary circumstances, and now there are more than ever before. the year 2023 will be the last year of the current financial perspective with full freedom for member states to spend money. but it should also be the year of the launch of the partnership agreements, that is, the launch of the new financial perspective, and the full programming of the national recovery plans. there will be new sources of funding, but of course there is a new circumstance, there is a war of unprecedented scale, a humanitarian disaster and a famine, most likely in north africa. we are not able to estimate the financial and budgetary consequences of this disaster that putin has prepared for us, but we all have the impression that our current resources are disproportionate to the needs. of course, the war does not undo the european union's key priorities that we know: the green deal, the digital economy, post-pandemic recovery, but the focus inevitably shifts to broadly defined security, strategic sovereignty, and independence from russian resources. we know from experience that crises are not conducive to the community method. the temptation then arises to conclude ad hoc intergovernmental agreements, and we will therefore all the more ensure that the role of the european parliament as the budgetary authority is properly appreciated and respected in these particular times.
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the protection of the environment and the environment-related aspects of the environment. every euro we put together in the common european basket must reduce the impact of the pandemic and the war in ukraine. that is why our political group has called for the european budget for next year to protect citizens against rising energy and food prices, to finance the sectors affected, especially tourism and agriculture, and the creation of new companies, to support programmes in the fields of innovation, education, health and culture. in this regard, we have integrated the following proposals into the european parliament's common position: additional support for the member states bordering ukraine, but also for the republic of moldova; the implementation of a joint programme to support the elderly; the development of the european medical corps for emergency situations; the creation of a compensation mechanism for the effects of war in order to be able to apply new sanctions against the russian federation; the settlement of the european investment programmes for ukrainian refugees to allow them access to education, health and social services; the financing of investments in the energy sector to reduce dependence on the russian federation. added to this is the need to ensure adequate resources for the protection of the population in emergencies and for international humanitarian action, while preserving our defining democratic values. but new resources are needed to meet these challenges, including by making existing programmes more flexible and extending programmes and implementation periods. it's not enough to just make transfers from existing resources. people expect us to allocate budgetary resources where they're needed and to deliver results quickly. we call on the european commission and the council to accelerate the joint response to today's challenges, ensuring that funds get faster to where they are needed.

madam president, commissioner, colleagues, thank you, nicolae ştefănuţă, neither the next generation eu nor the flexibility of the 2021-2027 mff are sufficient for the financial needs generated by russia's aggression against ukraine. the eu must respond on several fronts at the same time, without reversing european political and structural priorities for a greener, more digital, more social europe. the response to refugees, to the supply chains of essential goods and raw materials, to the supply and the brutal rise in energy prices, this response must be european, based on solidarity, it must come from the union budget! flexibility exists and is necessary, but it is not enough. commissioner, we have to start thinking about the revision of the 2021-2027 mff, yes, but the traditional revision is not enough. we need to look at the architecture of the qfp. it is time to equip the european union with the appropriate and agile instruments in the european union's financial framework for these and other future crises so that we are prepared for whatever comes.

madam president, the measures on energy issues announced by the council leave untouched the essential aspects of the energy market, including the pricing methodology on wholesale markets, which is responsible for the brutal differences between production costs and consumer prices, ensuring huge profit margins for private oligopolistic groups. the current situation highlights not only the need to regain public control of strategic sectors such as energy, but also to promote national production by reducing production deficits and the external dependence of states. the adoption of the so-called strategic compass shapes the militaristic path which the european union insists on pursuing, a path which absorbs increasing resources for this militaristic drift, while resources are lacking in combating the deficits in production, energy, food, technology and democracy, in fully financing public services, in combating poverty, injustices and social inequalities. this is a path which will not bring greater security to europe, but will bring greater risks. the escalation of arms and the policy of sanctions do not serve the interests of the peoples nor the necessary path to peace.

madam president, the proposal raised raises a number of doubts and concerns which do not allow us to follow it. i'm just listing a few that are not properly safeguarded. the ongoing digitisation of administrative law and the subsequent development of interoperability of systems, allowing the interconnection of databases with open access or with different levels of access, may constitute a risk associated with the concentration of data that should generally be avoided, which may compromise the right to the protection of personal data. the sharing of data between companies for remuneration, in a mercantilist logic of personal data, is contrary to the principle of looking at personal data as an extension of the human personality, subtracted from any forms of marketing, which aims to legitimize practices that are now illegal and legally void. the necessary assurance of consent in any communication of personal data should be exercised with due care and the introduction of a purported personal data sharing intermediary raises reasonable doubts as to its independence and civil liability. it is the preservation of the interest of the individual, not of the large multinationals, that should guide these initiatives.

on behalf of the verts/ale group. – madam president, strengthening cooperation in research and innovation worldwide is essential in tackling together global challenges such as climate change, delivering innovative solutions to them, and fostering societal progress on a global scale. reinforcing the eu’s leading role in this field is therefore a key dimension to our international action. while joining forces with key non-eu partners with a strong r and i base – we have to join forces with them – but also at the same time we need to scale up cooperation with other partners that maybe don’t have such a strong base, but where research and innovation has a huge potential to accelerate sustainable and inclusive development like, for instance, in africa. to do so efficiently, we need to be as open as possible. but this should not apply at all costs. we have to hold fast, as the main necessity, when fundamental values such as academic freedom, research ethics, gender equality or international law provisions are at stake. in that regard, we are witnessing a dangerous trend where academic freedom becomes more endangered. we need further action to counter this tendency, and that is why we are proposing in our resolution that the commission establishes a programme to support fellowships or stays for researchers that are currently at risk. like this, we can tackle attacks to academic freedom while attracting talent into the eu. finally, being open in our global approach to r and i has to be compatible with identifying and preserving our technological and scientific assets that are key to our strategic autonomy.

madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we have suffered a great deal during the pandemic. but we've also learned a lot about our lives, the challenges we face, the weaknesses we have, but also the resources we can rely on to overcome these challenges. and above all, i think, at all levels, there is a very strong awareness that has been created in all of us, of how much our lives really depend on research, on science, on the efforts of researchers, of individuals, women and men, sometimes even very young, who apply themselves with the ability to turn these results into applications and to the whole momentum of innovation. it was a strong realization: we have had days when our freedom to move and to act depended on the words of scientists, on what they told us, on what they discovered, on the assurances they gave us. now, we must not forget this role and this and this force that science represents for people's lives. that is why the horizon programme is so important: the commission's proposal is one of the central pillars of our european action. but we must be clear: the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. that is why the programme is important and why we as a parliament will support it.
madam president, if we are to talk about research and innovation, we must necessarily talk about its agents, the researchers and their working conditions. many of them are uncertain about the renewal of their research grant, the term of their contract, the outcome of the competition and the financing of a project. what they need is stability in a bond. to publish the results of their research, specialist publishers, who live off the work of these researchers, even ask for payment for the publication of articles. in other words, researchers, often under pressure to evaluate their position, produce science which they publish in scientific papers, which are either given away free of charge or paid for to publishers, who in turn turn turn turn to scientific reviewers free of charge and then sell these papers to other researchers and institutions. we need open access policies and the guarantee that institutions and researchers will not have any kind of cost to publish and access scientific papers, ensuring that it is not the publishers who profit from the scientific work of researchers. a final note: the sharing of scientific knowledge and its application should be in the service of humanity and the well-being, peace and health of peoples, rather than being hindered or even prohibited.

the history of the roma in europe is a traumatic history of persecution, segregation and misunderstanding. in november i took part in a mission of the committee on employment and social rights to investigate the situation of roma camps in slovakia. the horror of what i witnessed there will never leave my memory. thousands of people are treated in an unworthy manner, living in squalid tents, without any comfort or privacy, without drinking water, without conditions to warm themselves and, in some cases, in the vicinity of garbage dumps. the failure to integrate these communities cannot continue to exist outside the scope of the european institutions. these people are deprived of access to healthcare and children are deprived of access to education, which will lead to the perpetuation of intergenerational poverty and extreme social exclusion. although less dramatic, the stigma and exclusion of roma communities is also found in several other european countries, including my own country, portugal. in many cases, roma are treated as second-class citizens, who are excluded from the european values we so much want to proclaim and for whom the european social model is a mirage. many survive as permanent refugees in their own country. the european union and the member states have done something, but we have to do much more, much better and much faster. in the context of the next generation eu and the multiannual financial framework, it is imperative that the commission demands the definition of measures that contribute to the integration of these people at all levels: decent housing, education, health, employment, training. and then it is essential that the implementation of these measures be monitored on the ground. we cannot accept the perpetuation of the stigma against roma communities, which is contrary to the founding values of our union and the principles of humanism. the european union must guarantee dignity to all people.

the european commission has repeatedly stated since the end of 2019 that albania has fulfilled all the necessary requirements to move to the next stage of negotiations in its accession process to the european union. in the commission's report for 2019 and 2020, the recommendation to mark the first intergovernmental conference is clear, a recommendation also shared by this parliament in the subsequent report adopted in 2021. we recognised at that time that, despite the need to continue implementing reforms, the criteria for marking the first conference had already been met. despite this, we are almost halfway through the year 2022 and albania is still waiting. since then, the country has not been idle. it has continued to make progress on its path towards european integration, but the blockade on the union side has created a feeling of frustration among the population and the authorities, which threatens to affect the country and the very deepening of the european project. it's time to end this impasse. the first intergovernmental conference has to be postponed until the end of the french presidency of the council and i regret that the presidency is not represented here in this debate. it was very important that this happened. in this report, which we shall be voting on tomorrow, i stress the importance of moving forward with the accession process, so as not to disappoint the expectations that have been created and to encourage the continuation of the implementation of the reforms in the country. this text, enriched by the contribution of my fellow shadow rapporteurs, whom i thank for their excellent cooperation, was approved by the afet committee with more than 90% of the votes in favour, which strengthens the recommendations contained in it. among these recommendations, i would like to highlight the attention paid to relations between the different actors in albanian society, first of all with regard to the dialogue between the different political parties and actors in the country, which has made considerable progress since the last elections, but which can and should be improved. the role of the media and civil society should be strengthened and any legislative initiative on the media should follow the guidelines of the venice commission. i would also like to recall the need, highlighted in the report, to close the remaining electoral loopholes before the next general elections, in accordance with the osce/odih recommendations. the report highlights the importance of the developments already made and what still needs to be done in the energy and environmental protection sectors, among others, as well as the efforts that have been made in the fight against trafficking in human beings, arms and goods and the good cooperation in these areas with the european agencies. i would remind you that albania is the only country in the region which has signed cooperation agreements with all the european union agencies in the field of justice and home affairs. i also stress, as does the report, the work of the special structure for combating corruption and organised crime, as well as the progress achieved in implementing a comprehensive reform of the justice system, with the implementation and continuation of an unprecedented verification process and the re-establishment of the functioning of the constitutional court and the high court. in conclusion, i would like to stress here that albania has over time proved to be a reliable partner in foreign policy matters. this has been seen recently, once again, in support for ukraine, in sanctioning russia and in monitoring the positions of eu member states in the united nations general assembly, as well as in their membership of the security council. albania and north macedonia will still have to go through a long process of reform, it is true, but it is an unshakable fact that all the conditions for the first intergovernmental conference have already been met. the accession process for albania cannot be held hostage to parallel discussions. it's time to move on.

on behalf of the left group. mr president, the problem of tax evasion by multinational companies has been growing for many decades and has benefited from the support of right-wing political groups and countries whose public finance models are based on the expropriation of tax revenue that rightfully belongs to other states. this opposition was very clear, both in the negotiation of the oecd agreement and in the debate on this report, in which it was chosen because the right wing wanted it to be so, simply by stamping the international agreement of the oecd. this agreement is an important precedent which, we understand, only happens because of the growing threats, or even concrete, unilateral measures, taken by states to protect themselves and to protect their tax revenue from theft by other member states, in the case of the european union, or simply states. but it is a very imperfect law, which has been promoted with a very misleading advertising campaign and which we will have to revisit when it is revealed and when it becomes clear that it is deeply inadequate. precedent is important. the law will have to be much improved.
madam president, taxes are the most important source of public revenue and a manifestation of the sovereign power of states. it is taxes that finance our budgets and it is the tax burden that determines better or worse conditions for companies to create jobs, generate wealth and contribute to our economic growth. tax competitiveness is therefore an unavoidable dimension of any economic strategy. but this competition must be fair, balanced and, of course, transparent. the international agreement on the taxation of multinationals, in particular this second pillar on the effective minimum rate, was an important step. the union has led the negotiations and will lead the implementation of the agreement. the time for discussion must be transformed into the time for action. the commitment of member states is essential to ensure that this directive is adopted, is properly transposed and is applied in a way that safeguards the competitiveness of our economies and our ability to attract investment. unfortunately, the union is not being accompanied on the outside by the commitment of other countries, and on the inside we have yet to decide on the first pillar, the rules which guarantee taxation of profits generated on our territory. we have a lot of work to do and we need to do more. and this parliament will continue to demand that.
mr president, the implementation of the oecd global agreement in the european union is not only historic but also a huge responsibility. historic because it opens the way to a global solution and greater fiscal justice. historic because it is an important step towards reducing the 190 billion euros the european union loses annually in tax evasion and aggressive tax planning. a sum that would allow us to fund nextgenerationeu every four years. but it is also a responsibility to continue to provide the european union with new own resources, which are essential to finance the common investments needed to meet the challenges. without the creation of new own resources, the financing and debt repayment of instruments such as next generationeu or other instruments needed now or in the future, for example as a result of the war in ukraine, could put enormous pressure on european budgets and lead to an unsustainable increase in national contributions or cuts in european policies.
in writing. this proposal for a directive on a minimum level of taxation for multinational groups defines, of course, what does not exist today. but setting a minimum effective tax rate of 15% is very limiting, as it does not reverse the path followed by all eu countries, some more than others, of low taxation of large economic groups. this is clearly a political option and there is a risk that the alleged minimum level will become a maximum tax reference. moreover, to consider only multinational groups with a consolidated turnover of more than eur 750 million in at least two of the last four consecutive fiscal years is to leave out of the directive about 90%-85% of multinational groups! at the same time, it also excludes parent companies that are pension funds, investment funds or real estate investment vehicles. in other words, famous vulture funds or those companies that have contributed significantly to speculation in the real estate market are not covered by this directive. in the end, this directive is nothing more than an exercise in mixing, reshuffling and (almost) keeping everything the same and, blatantly, it still tries to interfere in a matter of member states' sovereignty such as taxation.
madam president, the outermost regions face permanent constraints on their development, as recognised in article 349 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union. i therefore welcome the renewed strategy for the outermost regions presented by the european commission on 3 may. a people-first strategy that takes into account the specific challenges faced by these regions, such as madeira and the azores in portugal, such as employment rates below the eu average, especially among young people, the high level of people at risk of poverty and population decline. i therefore call on my colleagues to take immediate action for the outermost regions, which will promote effective social and territorial cohesion through air and sea connectivity, employment and access to training, and derogations in the environmental transition, which will enable the outermost regions to adapt fully.
madam president, the execution by the state of israel of shireen abu akleh would, in any other context, be a worldwide scandal, not least because it was followed by more attacks after the journalist was dead. first, at his funeral for flying his country's flag, and then with a smear campaign in the israeli press. however, shireen's autopsy also revealed the hypocrisy of the european institutions and their deafening silence on a matter that, in any other context, would have deserved a strong and unanimous condemnation. it must be said that those who condemn occupations and not others, who protect lives and not others, do not defend peace. defend one side, and that support will cost you lives. but peace was defending shireen until the day she was murdered. we shall never see it again, except in the faces of those who have not stopped resisting and denouncing a criminal occupation. even without the support and solidarity of the european institutions which preach the values of human rights so much and practice them so little.
