madam president, working class households are experiencing unbearable price rises and shortages of electricity, fuel and consumer goods, with the risk of a food crisis. they are experiencing the results of the green transition strategy, the imperialist conflict in ukraine and the competition and sanctions that have been imposed. with subsidies and loans for common orders, the people will repay the profitability of the energy groups. the commission's recommendations and those of governments place the individual responsibility on the people, charging them even for wasteful use of electricity. they are imposing the interests of the eu energy monopolies that do business with russia. at the same time, they are rigging the supply of expensive american lng, with greek shipowners hoarding twice as much, transporting it as well as russian oil. the people must not become a punching bag in the european union, the united states and nato's competition with capitalist russia; they must strengthen their struggle for their own interests, for disengagement from imperialist plans and war equipment by lifting sanctions, abolishing indirect taxes and real increases.
madam president, while we are debating this in the european parliament, the conference of the outermost regions is taking place in martinique; the outermost regions are the only ones mentioned by name in european law, in the treaty of lisbon, precisely to protect their particularities: six french regions, including martinique, two portuguese regions and one spanish region, the canary islands. and they are debating the european union's strategy for the outermost regions presented by the commissioner for cohesion and reforms, elisa ferreira. and that's okay, we should celebrate. but its defence will not be complete until special treatment is asserted in two european laws which are being debated by this house. the first is the imposition of a minimum rate of interest on the profits of large multinationals. because this parliament is being consulted, but it will be up to the national governments, during the final debate on the directive, to determine the specific features of the tax systems of some of these outermost regions. the second is the temporary adjustment to the decarbonisation of the economy so that it does not impose untenable conditions on regions that are entirely dependent on their air connectivity and maritime supply.
madam president, last friday tatjana ždanoka, a member of this parliament, was arrested in riga with other protesters who were demonstrating publicly and peacefully against the demolition of a monument to soviet soldiers in victoria park. his detention deserves condemnation from this parliament. we hereby express our solidarity with tatiana and with those who do not give up defending the historical truth, the rights and freedoms that the struggle of the peoples has guaranteed. the same european union which seeks to assert itself as the champion of human rights and democracy in different parts of the world is living in harmony with these violations or even promoting them. the european union's imposition of censorship on media outlets, which is now extending to cultural and sporting activities, the removal of national powers and laws, such as the constitution of the portuguese republic, imposes a logic of single thinking. the destruction of historical heritage is another face of historical revisionism and the attempt to rewrite history, aiming to erase the achievements of the soviet union and the communists and the rights and gains achieved by the struggle of the peoples.
mr president, the atrocities we have witnessed in bucha and a little bit throughout ukraine, committed by the russian invading forces, remind us of the scenarios we have already seen in chechnya, syria and many other places. and they are a mirror of the state of affairs that impunity has been leading us to. and this is a fight we all have to do: the fight against impunity! to this end, it is essential that europol and eurojust be given the means and mandate to act vigorously and with full efficiency in the collection and protection of evidence and that we support the international criminal court in its work. but this is not enough. it is not enough to try war crimes and crimes against humanity. the crime of aggression must also be tried and that crime of aggression can only be tried in a special court. it is therefore important that the european union, here too, plays a clear and visible supporting role in the establishment of this court. but we also need to take care of the victims, the women who have been raped, and ensure that they have access to abortion and access to their sexual and reproductive health in conditions that uphold their dignity.
mr president, the european union's sanctions policy sacrifices the interests of the countries and peoples of europe and contributes to the escalation of confrontation, making the path to peace more difficult. the case of gas is a paradigmatic one. the sanctions package adopted is followed by an even sharper speculative increase in the price of gas, which had already been rising in previous months, and the people have to pay. gas storage targets are set for member states to meet, but as economic groups do not want to make these investments, it is the states and the people who will pay. some dependencies are replaced by others, more distant and environmentally harmful, more expensive, and people pay for it. it is clear that state intervention and the establishment of ceilings is necessary, but it is not enough. it is necessary to go further. the exploration, procurement, production, transport and marketing of the different forms of energy must be in the public domain, under public and democratic scrutiny. it is the only way that the people will not pay for the war, the sanctions and the profits and the exploitation of the multinationals in the sector.
mr president, i acknowledge the european commission's effort in presenting this repowereu. it is necessary and it is the moment. but repowereu is revolutionising, to put it appropriately, several key european instruments, in particular the multiannual financial framework 2021-2027 and the recovery and resilience facility. that is why we are calling on the european commission to come forward with the necessary proposals, which are clear and transparent and which safeguard european policies, priorities and defined objectives. the revision of the 2021-2027 mff is therefore forthcoming. and we want a thorough debate on repowereu in this parliament. we can adjust european priorities. we cannot second-guess well-defined political objectives. and one thing is certain: under no circumstances will we accept the devaluation of the role of the european parliament, particularly as budgetary authority.
mr president, commission president, i should like to begin by congratulating the rapporteur. today, as always, the european union needs allies to meet challenges in the areas of security, combating disinformation, foreign interference, protecting critical infrastructure, securing supply chains, combating climate change, ocean governance and preserving the planet's biodiversity. the challenges affect both the european union and the indo-pacific and require strong cooperation guided by european values. the european union must step up its efforts to promote human security and eradicate poverty, social injustice and human rights violations. it is essential that the european union enhances security cooperation with partners in the indo-pacific region, recognising the diversity of the region, i.e. a one-size-fits-all approach would not be the appropriate strategy.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, today's debate unfortunately shows that european cohesion policies have not been able to deal effectively in recent years with the various socioeconomic problems of the 20 million citizens living on the islands of the european union. this is a matter for serious reflection, since it was on the small island of ventotene in the mediterranean that the european project began some 80 years ago with the manifesto of altiero spinelli, which envisaged the creation of a europe which was politically, socially and territorially cohesive, a europe in which all citizens could enjoy the same rights and services. but today, as my colleagues have said, islands, especially the smaller ones, suffer from structural disadvantages: the islands are characterised by low population density, difficult access to energy and water, poor access to health and cultural services, dependence on transport and an economy almost exclusively based on tourism, fishing or agriculture, despite the potential of the islands. this resolution represents an important step towards the definition of a strategy that can bridge the socioeconomic gap between islands and continental areas, guaranteeing their development prospects in view of the challenges of the green and digital transition, but above all by implementing a concrete european cohesion policy, as provided for in article 174 of the treaty on the functioning of the european union.
mr president, my first words of thanks to mr omarjee for presenting this timely report and my thanks also to the commission. 20 million inhabitants, 4.6% of the union's population, represent our citizens living on islands. 2400 islands from 13 member states. i come from mallorca (balearic islands, spain). there has long been a recognition that insularity is a permanent structural disadvantage and needs to be compensated for. we have many challenges living on islands: size, population pressure, exceptionalism, double or triple insularity on occasion, dependence on air transport, physical disconnection from the mainland, the costs of exports and imports, difficulties in the labour market, difficulties in being able to access education on equal terms, difficulties in accessing health and water and energy. in the case of tourism, the balearic islands are practically dependent on tourism. we need additional fiscal support to be able to have sustainable tourism. the same applies to agriculture; agriculture is also very important and we need a specific plan to be competitive. we have made an appeal to the islands from the balearic islands for this or that, in favour of agriculture. i would also like to say that it is very important to abolish the minimum rule, because aid to alleviate insularity can never be state aid: it is aid to enable them to be equal. we ask nothing to be above anyone; we ask what we ask to be equal.
mr president, one of the main challenges facing the eu's islands, which are becoming more frequent, is the extreme natural phenomena and frequent flooding caused by climate change, which are devastating because of the lack of sustainable management and conservation of agricultural and forestry soils. in this respect, the european union solidarity fund must be improved to deal more realistically with the consequences, as this report itself acknowledges. we need a specific support mechanism to allow for greater eligibility for the outermost regions of the european union, with the possibility of this fund being mobilised to finance emergency operations and recovery in the event of damage of less than 1% of gdp, when affected in a small area, taking into account and considering its specific location and its resources.
madam president, as has been said many times, the islands of the european union as a whole face permanent structural disadvantages, such as their small size, often combined with very difficult topography, low population density, which is also reflected in small internal markets and a heavy dependence on local products, which they can only mitigate through another huge dependence: that of sea and air transport. in addition to structural disadvantages, our islands, particularly the outermost ones, are still at the forefront of the regions most affected by climate change, suffering from an increase in the frequency and intensity of natural disasters with enormous impact on their economy, their ecosystems, not to mention the cases where they even claim human lives. it is therefore essential to draw up a specific strategy for the islands of the european union, with clearly defined priorities for action, in order to reduce the impact of their structural disadvantages and to promote the development of measures to combat, prevent and adapt to the new challenges they face.
madam president, commissioner, i think we are dealing with a very interesting report, with 93 points, all of them of great interest. i would like to stress that the most important thing in today's debate is to put the islands at the centre. we need a european pact for the islands because otherwise there are no specific policies. the only one that existed was posei, and we saw in the reform how they wanted to cut their budget. i do not know what will happen in 2027, whether it will be a policy within the cap or a state policy. the islands have characteristics and situations which require specific support, whether they are outermost islands or not. i am from spain, with the balearic and canary islands, which are the outermost regions, and which require specific measures. let us also be sensitive to the objective 55 package tomorrow, which is not at all sensitive, by the way, to these islands.
the reality of island regions within the european union is diverse and presents structural disadvantages compared with continental territories, due to their size, low population density, seasonality, high topographical variation or poor connectivity. these structural features require a positive distinction between public investment policies which enable greater territorial and social cohesion and counteract the divergent trend in the development of these territories. the portuguese islands are also ultraperipheral regions, suffering from this double insularity. the development of the archipelagos of the azores and madeira necessarily requires investment in agriculture, support for small-scale and traditional fishing, industrial development, sustainable tourism and improved accessibility, both between islands and with the mainland. it also involves policies and investments to achieve this development, in particular a posei dedicated to fisheries and another to transport, as we have advocated and will continue to advocate. we regret that the conditionality imposed on the structural funds is having an impact on these regions, limiting development possibilities which would be fundamental.
madam president, when president von der leyen announced her ambitious green agenda and her intention to put the european union in the global leadership of the fight against global warming, nobody said it was going to be easy. but a clear majority in this european parliament supports that undeniable objective. we also call for a just transition to a carbon-free economy, fair to vulnerable sectors and fair to island and remote territories such as the outermost regions, which have a specific legal basis for special treatment that allows them a reasonable time to assimilate the objective, so that it is not unattainable. i would therefore like to thank those who will be voting on the amendments on the uprs in the emissions trading directive in this strasbourg plenary, because i am convinced that this will give them the timeframe modulated and adjusted to their needs to incorporate renewable energies and to meet the objective of a sustainable, green, blue, circular and innovative economy.
mr president, for over a year we have been warning about the loss of purchasing power. and in the meantime, we're caught up in reality. and it's not just putin, and it's not just the disruption in supply chains. it is the reality of excessive government spending, a war on fossil fuels, corporate regulatory pressure and the printing of money by the european central bank. that is the root of the problems of high inflation. but what does the eu do? more of the same: a green deal with more rules, more taxes, the corona recovery fund a well-paid cigar from its own box , which the netherlands throws away to reduce nitrogen emissions and offshore wind energy. the dutch are getting the boot on top of car taxes, carbon taxes and airline taxes. president, a fall in purchasing power calls for less taxation, less regulation, less government and less eu. instead, progressive europe is waging a war against the free market, a war against our savers, pensioners and young people. this policy must stop.
madam president, the deterioration of living conditions, the increase in housing costs, the loss of purchasing power of households and the rise in interest rates make it urgent to protect the homes of many families and prevent them from becoming homeless. in this sense, states should assume the role of promoters of public housing and of promoters of policies of construction and urban rehabilitation in order to expand the supply of public housing at costs compatible with household incomes. we advocate increased public investment in the rehabilitation and construction of public housing, which responds to the housing shortages identified, to workers and, in particular, to young people, enabling them to become self-sufficient and to start a family. the supply of public housing under the supported or conditional rent schemes needs to be increased. but this requires breaking away from the budgetary constraints imposed by the european union, followed by national governments which, as we can see, limit the responses needed to improve the living conditions of the people.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to combat fraud. mr president, mr vice-president, madam minister, i begin by greeting all the rapporteurs of this package entitled objective 55. we cannot have the most vulnerable, the already victims of energy poverty, paying for the climate transition. we have ambitious climate targets, and we have to have them, but we need to promote social justice here as well. that's why the social climate action fund is necessary. but this is new expenditure in the budget of the european union, and new expenditure means new money. it cannot be created at the expense of european programmes and policies, and particularly cohesion policy. it must respect the democratic principles of the european union budget: transparency, universality, democratic control by the european parliament. the committee on budgets proposes an adjustment mechanism in the event of a carbon market development that benefits citizens and member states.
madam president, mr vice-president timmermans, minister zacharopoulou, europe cannot give up its ambition to lead the fight against climate action. we owe it to the younger generations, whose quality of life is threatened by the catastrophic consequences of global warming. i support the unavoidable measures that aim to reduce carbon emissions by 55% by 2030, so as to achieve carbon neutrality by 2050. it's the only way to stop the accelerating rise in temperature in our common home, planet earth. measures are essential to reconcile the energy transition with competitiveness and social cohesion. with appropriate funding, the energy transition will provide a stimulus for technological innovation and its rapid incorporation into industry and everyday life. the transition is compatible with economic progress and, in this difficult time of war in europe, it must contribute to our strategic autonomy. increased energy efficiency and increased use of renewable energy means less dependence on fossil fuels. the transition may, in the short term, have a negative impact on the lives of many families and many businesses. it is therefore essential to create the social climate action fund, financially supporting households and their energy consumption, reducing public transport expenditure and helping small businesses. we need that fund to have a much larger financial allocation, because the climate transition has to be socially fair, respecting the values of the european union and ensuring the essential support of the citizens.
mr president, mr vice president, i have to say that i am very pleased. solving the trilemma of price, supply and environmental policy is not easy. but in this equation it is vital not to increase costs for individuals, industries and smes. the commission has also proposed that the council should adopt a decision on the conclusion of the agreement on the european economic area. that is why we should support the stabilization of the co2 price by injecting some of the rights from the stability reserve into the market in the vote tomorrow. moreover, it is crucial to allow more time to adapt the border adjustment mechanism before limiting the free allocation of allowances. finally, as we approved in the committee on industry, the social climate fund should be available to all smes, especially those affected by the new rules on emissions trading. is there an urgent need to respond to the challenges of climate change? - i'm not sure. but we must do so without adding costs that would suffocate the economy.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your report. first of all, i would like to thank my friend david casa and esther de lange for what they have presented to the social climate fund. i believe that it is an extremely important contribution to support those who are most affected by change, by transformation, in this difficult period. i believe that the fight against climate change is perhaps the greatest socioeconomic challenge of our century. that is not disputed by anyone except a few clerks who are not even here to participate in the debate. but the present situation, the war in ukraine, inflation, shows us in what direction the development is going. and we have to keep in mind that we are still ahead of the green deal, ahead of implementation, ahead of fit for 55! we are seeing a rise in prices, particularly energy prices, which is simply threatening to many people. in germany, the model bill: up to eur 5000 additional burden for a family of four these are dramatic developments. and that is why it is important: we cannot solve every problem here at european level, but we are making an important contribution to it.
mr president, madam minister, i would like to thank you for your reply. my group is committed to the common goal of making our continent climate-neutral by 2050. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. we must ensure that production and innovation continue to take place in the eu. the co2 compensation mechanism at our borders is a screwdriver that can only work if we allow our industry to modernise itself through the proceeds of the cbam. and yes, that's why we can't let free vouchers expire prematurely. the commission, mr timmermans, likes to talk about open strategic autonomy. i am in favour of this concept. but if we fail to strike the right balance at the heart of our climate policy, we buy ourselves a clear conscience at best, but we slip into a foolish nimby dependency. let's stop this together!
mr president, the target of reducing emissions by 55% by 2030 is a very ambitious one, but also feasible, and i think most of us have taken this target on board, and i, as a romanian mep and chair of the committee on industry, research and energy, have promoted it as a key objective and will continue to promote it. but this can only be achieved by encouraging and implementing innovative technologies in the fields of energy, environment, industry and agriculture. what we legislate with this package will have an impact on the economy and on the citizens, and we must be careful to avoid putting even more pressure on an economy already so affected by the energy price crisis, the pandemic and, recently, russia's illegal aggression against ukraine. we need balance, we need to take account of the different starting points and the extent of the efforts and reforms that each member state will have to undertake. that's why i'm still in favor of the modernization fund being increased. we support the financing of natural gas projects as well as investments in nuclear energy.
mr president, mr vice-president, madam minister, in strengthening this system we cannot forget the outermost regions which are exclusively dependent on air and sea links for the transport of people and goods. if this is the case, a carbon tax will mean higher prices on the shelf and higher costs of living in general in regions that are already among the poorest in the eu. in this sense, it may also jeopardise their economic development and the level of employment in these regions, and hence the importance of ensuring special derogations to allow for full adaptation and a sustainable transition. i therefore call on everyone to take account of the specific features of the outermost regions, as is safeguarded by article 349 of the treaties, in particular and specifically when travelling between their member state, other member states, but also between islands, as is the case with the azores and madeira archipelagos in portugal. only then will we ensure effective territorial cohesion and a truly just transition that leaves no one behind.
mr president, vice-president timmermans has said here that this package for reducing emissions should leave no one behind. i said that knowing that the insistence on perverse market solutions, which are not only questionable in terms of their effectiveness in reducing emissions but also burden workers and populations, contributing to the increase in the cost of living, as the commission itself acknowledges, justifies the creation of the social fund for climate action to mitigate the socioeconomic consequences of the extension of the emissions trading scheme. this fund is clearly insufficient and the commission's proposal is marked precisely by support for a very restrictive range of those affected, leaving many behind. and the methodology for calculating the financial allocation particularly harms one member state, portugal. portugal is the only country whose gross national income per capita is less than 90% of the eu-27 average that will have an allocation of the total percentage of the fund that is less than the percentage of its population in the eu-27 as a whole. it is an unjustifiable discrimination which must be corrected.
mr president, mr vice-president, madam minister, the situation is dramatic. transport accounts for about 25% of total emissions in the european union and shows no downward trend. co2 performance standards for cars and vans have been a crucial element in limiting emissions from cars driving on our roads, but they are insufficient to respond to the climate emergency. and the revision that the commission has proposed to respond to the calls of citizens, science and the commitments made in the climate law is a good starting point, improved in this parliament in the report we are going to vote on tomorrow. it is extremely worrying, however, the lack of ambition shown by this house, which ignores the needs of the population, industry and our planet. we need to steer the sector towards decarbonisation in order to increase the supply of zero-emission technologies, reduce prices and rapidly create a second-hand and third-hand market for zero-emission light vehicles. if it is certain that the transition will have impacts, it is also certain that there are support mechanisms to mitigate them, just as we ask the commission to come forward with further support measures if necessary. i would remind you that this is an area where technological and economically viable solutions already exist. we cannot continue to allow him to be dismissed as irresponsible. i call on the members of this house to support the end date for internal combustion engines in 2035 and that we only have new zero-emission cars on our roads after that.

in writing. three decades ago, the erasmus programme, one of the european union's most important mechanisms, was born. if this was a programme aimed at exchanging experiences between young people, it should also be a programme that promotes inclusion, diversity, equal opportunities and accessibility. the covid-19 pandemic has had a negative impact on education and, consequently, on the implementation of this programme. however, recent measures adopted by the european commission in the 2021-2027 framework will increase diversity and inclusion in the current erasmus+ and european solidarity corps programmes. making the current programme more inclusive, with the capacity to reach the most vulnerable and disadvantaged people, and further facilitating the participation of local and smaller organisations, in particular the outermost, island, mountain and rural and less accessible regions, by adapting it to the current social and economic contexts, should be a priority. only in this way will it be possible to safeguard a fair distribution of resources and projects in each member state and to provide equal opportunities for all, including the most vulnerable and disadvantaged groups, so as to leave no one behind.
in writing. the erasmus+ regulation, for the period 2014-2020, wanted to focus on promoting social inclusion and participation of people with special needs or fewer opportunities, as defined in the erasmus+ programme's inclusion and diversity strategy, covering people with disabilities, health problems, educational difficulties, cultural differences and economic, geographical and social obstacles. erasmus is one of the mobility programmes that attracts young people for exchanges. over the years, the programme has been modified, but it remains not accessible to all. without the financial backing to finance the period outside their country of origin (tuition fees, accommodation, food), only those with the greatest financial resources benefit from this mobility. for students with the greatest financial difficulties who are already displaced in their own country, erasmus is a mirage indeed. one of the obstacles to accessibility to the erasmus programme is precisely the problem of housing, which this report does not mention. the lack of university housing and the astronomical prices faced by displaced students and families, together with the low value of scholarships, are impediments to experiencing this experience during the period of study. there have been improvements, but we need to make sure that the programme is really accessible to everyone.

member of the commission. – madam president, my colleague executive vice-president dombrovskis addressed this chamber on behalf of high representative / vice-president borrell on this issue on 6 june. since then, on 24 june, the us supreme court overruled the right to abortion established in the roe v. wade decision of 1973. the june decision returns the right to regulate abortion completely to the state level. thirteen states have enacted trigger laws that have already or will soon limit or ban completely access to abortion. so it is estimated that all together, 20 states may enact total bans or severe gestational limits on abortion. president biden has condemned the ruling. he has indicated a preference for congress to codify federal privacy and abortion rights. his administration, including the departments of state and defense, are also exploring ways to ensure access to sexual and reproductive healthcare for service members, their families and their civilian workforce, as permitted by federal law. the ruling constitutes a rollback of a longstanding legal standard in the us concerning reproductive rights, impacting the lives of many women and in particular, those in the most vulnerable situations. it is a reminder that hard-won rights cannot be taken as a given anywhere. the eu is committed to the implementation of its obligations in relation to the human rights of girls and women arising from key frameworks, including the convention on the elimination of all forms of discrimination against women, the beijing platform for action, the cairo declaration on population and development and their respective outcome reviews. un treaty body jurisprudence has indicated that denying women access to abortion can amount to violations of the rights to healthcare, privacy, and in certain cases, the right to be free from cruel, inhumane and degrading treatment. the un human rights committee has confirmed that although state parties may adopt measures designed to regulate voluntary terminations of pregnancy, such measures must not result in violation of the right to life of a pregnant woman or girl or her other rights under the covenant. through cooperation actions, dialogue and other initiatives the eu supports the full enjoyment of sexual and reproductive health and rights in countries across the globe. this means enabling women and girls to ensure bodily autonomy, free choice over their bodies, free from discrimination, coercion and violence, and to lead healthy lives. in the eu action plan on human rights and democracy and the eu gender action plan iii the eu has committed to scaling up action to achieve gender equality, to protect and promote the full and equal enjoyment of all human rights by all women and girls and their socioeconomic empowerment free from all forms of discrimination, while applying a gender responsive approach. in the european union sexual and reproductive healthcare is a member state responsibility. while the commission fully recognises every person’s fundamental right of equal access to high-quality health care, all women across the eu should have adequate access to good-quality healthcare and treatment. sexual and reproductive health is part of it. one of the objectives of the eu4health programme is to improve and foster health in the union to reduce the burden of communicable and non-communicable diseases, amongst others, by promoting access to healthcare. this includes access to sexual and reproductive healthcare. so the programme may support member states’ actions to promote access to sexual and reproductive healthcare and to support integrated and intersectional approaches to prevention, diagnosis, treatment and care where such actions are included in the annual work programme of the eu4health programme. we also support regular exchanges between member states and stakeholders on gender equality topics, such as gender equality and health, under the mutual learning programme in gender equality. we continue to fund civil society organisations working on gender equality, including on sexual and reproductive health and rights. and we provide full support to member states’ efforts in implementing the united nations sustainable development goals relevant to women’s health: universal access to sexual and reproductive care, family planning and education. women living in the eu must be equal and free in all aspects of their lives to choose the life they want in order to live, thrive and participate in all aspects of society. strong women’s rights are an asset and a key feature of democracies. it is an achievement we must be proud of and nurture, as it can be easily lost.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. madam president, the decision of the supreme court of the united states of america to abolish women's right to a conditional abortion after 50 years is a huge blow to the protection of human rights on a global scale, because this right along with contraception (1965) and the decriminalization of same-sex relations (2003) are enshrined in the same article of the constitution of the united states of america. in addition, the judicial majority that resulted in the anachronistic regulation is in complete contrast to the american society that is in favor of the right with a percentage of 60%. but beyond its backwardness, the supreme court's decision is clearly dangerous, for it misjudged what is happening in the world today, including, for example, that 60% of 121 million "unintended" pregnancies end in abortion; whereas the decriminalisation of abortion does not act as a deterrent but rather leads to unsafe medical services for pregnant women; whereas bans and restrictions introduce inequalities between economically weak women who are unable to go to neighbouring countries to solve their problem; whereas complications during pregnancy or childbirth are responsible for the death of adolescents aged 15 to 19 years; the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the european union has an obligation to take decisions that unite and not divide. the member states should, in an organised manner and with due regard for subsidiarity, decriminalise abortion, combat sexual violence effectively, and educate adolescents about sexuality, contraception, pregnancy, medical services and postnatal care. the world is changing, ladies and gentlemen, and we in the european union of democratic values are looking to the next generations with seriousness and responsibility. fundamental human rights, secured by years of struggle, require and will be protected by us all the time.

i would like to thank the rapporteur, mrs cresson, for her excellent report, and also for the fact that she has been able to give us a very good report on the subject. she was from cape verde and had emigrated to portugal because the father of her children had disappeared without a word. she left the children with her grandmother on an island in santiago, and came to look for resources, cleaning houses in the city and its surroundings on the hour. when i found her, it was seven in the morning, but for her the day had started two hours earlier in a very poor neighborhood on the south bank of the tagus river where she lived, and she still had an hour of transportation to get to her first job. her day would end very late, with two more hours to go. we often speak in technical jargon of the sociological concept of intersectionality. for those of you who don't know what that means, this is exactly what i've just described to you: being a woman and at the same time a single mother, an immigrant from africa, uneducated, precarious and poor. no union to defend their rights, no life to have supportive friends, almost invisible. it is for this and many other reasons that we are here today to discuss women's poverty and how to reduce it. for my part, when i vote in favour of this report, i feel that i will be fulfilling at least part of the promise i made to margarida on that day in may 2019.
on behalf of the ecr group. - madam president! - i have to go. i'm not going to let you down. ladies and gentlemen! poverty in the european union has a woman's face. the poverty of women is due to a number of factors. it's not just lower wages and pensions, age discrimination, low-wage jobs and precarious conditions, but also economic violence, unpaid domestic work and single motherhood. as many as 85% of single parents are women. and one in four children in europe is at risk of poverty. women are said to be poverty managers, managing the household budget, looking for opportunities, extra employment, cutting costs. so, working for and investing in women is an investment in their families, and especially their children. it is women who are the future of europe. it's up to them, or us, to make its demographic growth. but in the context of ageing european societies, it is also worth remembering that they play a key role and dominate quantitatively in the health and care services sectors. i thank the rapporteur for her constructive and good work.

on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, commissioner, throughout our lives almost all of us will care for or be cared for by someone. it is time we recognized that this task is crucial to the well-being of the society in which we live. in the european case, taking into account demographic trends, this will be the case more and more and it is women who provide the vast majority of care tasks. let us hope that this situation will change. it is estimated that women spend, on average, 3.2 times as many hours as men on unpaid care duties. and all of this leaves 7.7 million women out of the labor force. our challenge, our great challenge, is how to change this paradigm, which is also cultural, which starts in the family and ends in the labor market. so we need to look at education, showing our boys that caring for others is not just a woman's thing. we must then value care tasks. ensure public investment wherever necessary. paying fairly for this work and giving dignity to these tasks, recognizing the rights of those who care. in portugal, for example, the informal carer statute has been established, which guarantees a support allowance, rest periods and access to training. i am glad to know that the commission will be ambitious in its strategy. we need a european pact for care, with the certainty that each of us will one day benefit from this effort.

mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i believe that there are three main areas in which we must act in the field of care. of course, that is a matter for the member states. i know very well the problem in my country and in the countries of the east. we have a problem, on the one hand, with people who do the act of caring, very often they are treated without dignity, they don't have a work contract. we know that from the east, a lot of people went to the west to get more money and do this act of care. on the other hand, we have the nursing problem. so many times, the act of care is not quality, and we have the infrastructure problem. commissioner, if we do not include in the european strategy the budget for allocating money for infrastructure, because, for example, in my country we do not have the capacity for elderly people who cannot be cared for by their families and who need these centres, homes for the elderly, where they can be properly cared for, we will not be able to solve the situation. i have been trying for several years to convince the commission that this should be allocated, and the answer we receive systematically from the commission, that elderly people should be cared for in the family, is absolutely absurd, because they are people who have no place in the family, who no longer have families, and for this we must allocate budgets from the social budget, from the social fund, so that we can have places for these people who deserve our respect until the last day of their lives.
mr president, for decades right-wing policy choices and the prevalence of the criteria of deficit and debt over social justice have dictated public disinvestment in the social functions of the state, the discharge of the state's responsibility for promoting the welfare and protection of the population and the commodification of care, both in health and education. the absence or lack of an adequate, universal and free public response to these issues has led to thousands of people, mostly women, taking on the informal and individual responsibility of caring for close relatives, shifting the full responsibility for the people they care for onto their shoulders, subjecting themselves to brutal physical and psychological overload and depriving them of their personal and professional lives. we advocate the strengthening or creation of robust public networks of proximity, adequately equipped with financial, technical, material, infrastructural and human resources, decent working conditions, career development prospects and fair wages, protection and support for informal carers with psychosocial support and social protection, training and empowerment. it is time for the states to take care of carers and the people they care for.

on behalf of the green group in the european parliament. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we are still dealing with deaths in brazil. bruno pereira and dom phillips are the latest casualties of this war waged by bolsonaro and his agribusiness allies, but there is a cause, above all, our demand for brazilian goods. that's why the amazon is burning, let's be clear. bruno had received death threats because of his work with indigenous peoples and their organization univaja, precisely to rid their forests of illegal loggers, miners, poachers and drug traffickers. that is why we need strong european rules to stop the import of products which cause deforestation and violate human rights. and so it is scandalous that the council has agreed to this to ensure loopholes and limit traceability requirements. in addition, indigenous peoples are the best guardians of the forest, so it is necessary to defend them by monitoring the renewal of land protection ordinances. finally, i believe that all member states should ratify the 1989 convention on indigenous and tribal peoples, the only international convention on the rights of indigenous peoples.

mr president, commissioner, the drought in the european union affects more than a third of the continent and today the picture is dramatic. for example, in portugal, according to the portuguese institute of the sea and the atmosphere, 66% of the territory is in extreme drought and 33% in severe drought. so more must be done. monitoring and prevention strategies, supported by bodies such as the european drought observatory; land assessment plans, which should identify the groups most vulnerable to these extreme weather events and include appropriate adaptation measures; and also measures to mitigate the risk of drought and heat waves. unfortunately, it is no longer enough to try to reverse the impact of climate change. we will have to learn to live with them, but continue to work to mitigate them and thus ensure the health of our planet and those who live on it.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, mr vice-president of the commission, we are in fact experiencing the worst drought since records began in some parts of europe, particularly in southern europe. a recently published study points to a frequency of dry winters in the iberian peninsula of 1 every 4 years. in portugal, the effects of climate change are clear. the average annual rainfall has decreased by 20 millimeters per decade and there has been an increase in the frequency of heat waves, which are the ones that fuel large fires. today, in my country, 96.3% of the territory is extremely and severely dry. in view of this situation, mr commissioner, it is imperative to think of structural solutions. i would therefore like the european commission to look to the future and define a european strategy for increasing surface water storage capacity. a strategy that allows member states to finance outside the common agricultural policy to complete and modernise existing irrigation systems and promote infrastructure such as dams, it has to be said, and connections between reservoirs that can increase the resilience of the system. nature and the climate, but also agriculture and animal production, are obliging us to do this.
mr. chairman, please! as we can see, heat waves are frequent, droughts are increasing, especially in the southern part of europe, which includes my country, slovenia, in the south-east, and agriculture and forestry are certainly the biggest victims of this phenomenon. i therefore agree with my colleagues who stress the importance of strengthening civil protection systems, of being prepared for forest fires and of reducing them as soon as possible, and of ensuring that water is properly managed in agriculture. i come from an area where we have combined on the one hand the construction of a hydroelectric power station and irrigation, which takes precedence over energy consumption, and in this way we are trying to solve problems. modern, low-water irrigation technology is certainly needed, as are suitable rotary kilns and genetic techniques to produce plants that are more resistant to drought.
mr president, commissioner, we are actually discussing a subject which affects people's lives: heat, drought, fires. in my country, in my county, they burned the wheat fields, they didn't just burn the forests, they burned the grain. what are those farmers doing? they have loans in the bank. the cost of bank loans has gone up, at least in my country. i'm left with nothing. we have a surveillance system, a civil protection mechanism, we also have a solidarity fund. the question is how do we get this money to the people affected as quickly as possible? and yes, now we're dealing with the effects. we have to go to the causes at the same time. but it's burning now and we have to put it out, commissioner. so i ask you on behalf of the farmers in romania, on behalf of the citizens of romania who have been without water for months. in whole areas it hasn't rained and the ground is just cracked and people are drinking water by the portion. so we need to act urgently, of course, in cooperation with the governments of the member states, but we need to act urgently now.
mr president, there is a recurring debate on drought, extreme weather events and their impact on various vital sectors, such as agriculture, livestock and other productive and economic activities. in my country, portugal, the extreme and severe drought situation may also have an impact on firefighting. but it seems that we are always chasing after the damage and mitigation measures, obviously, are not enough. preventive measures are needed, including means to enhance water storage capacity, adaptation in productive activities, associated with the definition of water use criteria that ensure public supply for human consumption, public health, the preservation of income, the safeguarding of means of production and the safety and maintenance of water reserves. concrete measures to increase water use and storage are therefore essential, and it is therefore urgent to strengthen and mobilise financial resources for the construction or reconstruction of reservoirs, in particular irrigation, which are essential for small and medium-sized farms and family farms and for the preservation of the countryside.
mr president, thank you very much to the rapporteur. when we adopt a regulation or a directive here, our mission is not fulfilled until much later, when an ex post impact assessment proves to us that our objectives have been achieved. unfortunately, we all know that this is not always the case and that sometimes the problem is with the original text, or because it is written in an unclear and complex way, or because it is just one piece of a puzzle that is difficult to put together, or because its fulfillment depends on too many administrative obligations. to prevent this from happening, as this report shows, the drafting of the law must be more involved by the citizens. tested ideas and proof-of-concepts pilots, the simplest language and technology should be used to facilitate law enforcement. better legislation is essentially about making sure that european legislation is effective, and that we are satisfied with the result of our work, our work here in parliament, the commission and the council.
mr president, sustainable forest management must take account of aspects such as economic viability, the promotion of the multifunctionality of forests and the sustainability of forest ecosystems. it should encourage forestry and prevent rural neglect, which leaves large areas of land to be abandoned and encourages the proliferation of forest monocultures in the service of the paper industry. the european union's policies and guidelines bear responsibility for the current state of our forests. underfunding, privatisation and the dismantling of public services and the dominance of timber monopolies have suffocated thousands of small-scale forest producers. the protection of forests requires other policies (agriculture, rural development and trade) in addition to the removal of constraints on public investment by states. timber production prices are a crucial issue in ensuring the interest and commitment of small and medium-sized owners in active forest management that defends the forest, promotes its multifunctionality, ensures the sustainability of forest ecosystems and fosters native species.

member of the commission. mr president, mr minister, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for this debate. but let me first of all say, once again, that any attempt to gain illegal access to private data is unacceptable, including when it is made by public authorities, including intelligence services. the union already has a strong legal acquis to protect privacy, we need to ensure that this acquis is implemented and applied in all member states. there is a national security exception in european law which allows for derogations from the application of european rules where appropriate. however, the use of this exception must be justified in each specific case, in particular in the light of the principle of necessity and proportionality. this should be subject to scrutiny by national parliaments, but also by independent data protection authorities and national judicial authorities. we condemn the illegal use of spyware, but the facts must first be established and qualified by the judiciary. the commission does not have access to individual files. the defence of the rule of law requires above all an independent, qualified and efficient judiciary, and we rely on national authorities to investigate the cases that have been revealed and the allegations that have been made and to restore the confidence of citizens. we expect the national authorities, and this is the case in greece, to provide us with full information on the investigations undertaken and the follow-up given, including by the judicial authorities. and i would like to remind you that at european level, we must continue to strengthen our privacy protection tools, such as the eprivacy directive. i hope that the trilogues can make progress on this point.
president-in-office of the council. – mr president, the promotion and protection of eu fundamental values is a major concern for the council, and we will continue to pay close attention to any concrete situations where those values may be at risk. judging from today’s discussion, there is no doubt that parliament is equally committed to ensuring the protection of the rule of law and the privacy of our citizens. concerning the pegasus scandal and the questions that were sent by the pega committee of inquiry, as raised by mr lenaers, we welcome the activities of the committee of inquiry concerning this important issue. the member states received a questionnaire from the european parliament that touches the heart of national competencies and their domestic situation. just to be precise, these questions were addressed to individual member states. that being said, there is indeed a discussion underway in the council at a technical level regarding the possibility of a common reaction to the pega questionnaire. in addition, of course, member states can continue sending the pega committee their individual replies. the european parliament will be informed as soon as possible in the coming weeks. the czech presidency will, for its part, continue its efforts to bolster our capacity to protect our citizens better from any attacks on their fundamental rights. as i mentioned before, our agreement on the nis2 directive is a concrete example of what we can do together to achieve a higher level of cybersecurity in the eu. we also look forward to important proposals that the european commission is going to present this week: the european cyber resilience act and the media freedom act, as commissioner reynders mentioned. we stand ready to continue this cooperation to discuss and advance on other proposals to further protect the fundamental rights of our citizens and the resilience of our democracies.

the next item is the report (a9-0221/2022) by mr niels fuglsang, on behalf of the committee on industry, research and energy, on the proposal for a european parliament and council directive on energy efficiency (recast) (com (2021) 0558 c9-0330/2021 2021/0203 (cod)).
member of the commission. – mr president, the proposal for the recast of the energy efficiency directive is of the utmost importance to our energy system for sustainability, affordability and energy security. we are just over 200 days since russia’s invasion of ukraine. apart from the human tragedy that putin has caused, the war has triggered volatility, and uncertainty on our energy markets and the threat of gas disruption this winter is a scenario to be ready for. at the same time, we have seen images of extreme weather affecting our lives and stressing our energy system, and this keeps repeating over and over again. both of these developments need a response and action that is even more robust than before. energy savings are the quickest and cheapest way to address the current energy crisis. this is key, as high energy costs are hitting our vulnerable consumers and businesses most. saving energy directly reduces our energy bills and makes our economy more resilient. energy efficiency means saving on energy prices, investment costs and system costs. but it needs to be done right – e.g. will complement the urgent action we are taking to reduce gas consumption in view of our repowereu goals, just like we already see with our ‘save gas for a safe winter’ plan. as you know, electricity demand reduction will be the starting point of the emergency intervention that we are currently finalising. beyond this winter, structural change at the very foundation of how we use energy every day will help end our dependence on russian fossil fuels faster and protect our economy. in this way, energy efficiency is a strategic tool to strengthen and safeguard our energy system. given its importance, i want to thank the rapporteur, nils fuglsang, and his colleagues from the itre committee for the important progress made on the proposal. you have shown a true commitment to pushing for the highest ambition in many of the provisions to effectively alleviate energy poverty. on the way forward, i would stress the importance of a robust and effective eeg, otherwise we risk undermining its impact, in particular when it comes to the provisions on public buildings and innovation and stopping support to fossil fuel technologies. i hope that we can stick to the spirit of the proposal. the current crisis and the challenging winter ahead will remain a constant reminder of the importance of our joint effort on this proposal, with a collective, constructive mind—set.
on behalf of the renew group. mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, it is difficult to sum up in a minute the result of months and months of work. the text we are going to adopt in the next few days is ambitious and concrete, without any compromise with ideology or political manifestos. managing the transition means choosing achievable goals. we then decided to increase our target of reducing our energy consumption to 40% by 2030 compared to 2007; pushed for binding national contributions, while leaving flexibility to member states for increased electrification and hydrogen production; ensuring that the public sector effectively plays an exemplary role in the efficiency of its real estate portfolio, while safeguarding the cultural heritage of cities; the commission also called on energy-intensive companies to invest in improving production processes through appropriate accompanying measures. the present situation requires us to make the time limit short, knowing that the cheapest energy that protects the environment and makes us less dependent on third parties is the energy we do not consume.
on behalf of the left group. mr president, we have no doubt about the potential for renewal and innovation in energy networks, buildings, transport and other public utilities, with a view to greater energy efficiency. however, by setting binding targets that require more effort from some states than others, the european union is shifting the responsibility and the financial effort required to achieve them onto the member states, which is neither fair nor feasible. we advocated that the funds mobilised to promote energy efficiency should be excluded from the calculation of the public debt and budget deficits of the member states, so that the necessary investments could be made, but our proposal was not accepted. we also believe that energy efficiency policies should not contribute to greater social exclusion and worsening of citizens' living conditions through higher housing costs, mobility or energy prices. we believe that public control of the energy sector is crucial to increasing energy efficiency and to guaranteeing the economic, social and environmental rights of the people.
the european parliament will vote tomorrow on the report on the european commission's proposal to revise the multiannual financial framework (mff 2021-2027). the commission has proposed, and rightly so, the creation of the social fund for climate action. this revision of the mff is now objectively necessary and urgent, but as we have already seen, in the new political and economic context - the war against ukraine and the social and economic consequences of the war on the european union and on the lives of citizens and businesses - we will have to go further in the near future, already next year. the budget of the european union is the most powerful instrument for meeting the political ambitions of the european union. and we already see that you have to have a permanent instrument that can be activated in times of crisis. we must use the lessons we have learned in recent times to make the budget of the european union more flexible and more compatible with regular times, but also with times of turbulence and uncertainty, always on the basis of solidarity and trust between institutions and member states and protecting citizens. this fund is intended to support the most vulnerable in the transition to climate neutrality. a fund that aims to absorb the disproportionate social impact that climate change will bring, by supporting households, micro-enterprises and tackling mobility poverty. this fund is even more vital today than when it was proposed by the commission. it can become a decisive instrument in combating inequalities in the process of building the energy autonomy of the european union. commissioner, parliament has started from the commission's proposal. but he did more! it has created an automatic mechanism to adjust for fluctuating carbon prices. this means that in the event of an increase in the carbon price, additional appropriations will be made available to the fund, ensuring that the appropriations available in the union budget increase in line with the carbon price. member states may programme this additional funding in priorities or measures they have already identified or in new priorities. in summary, the proposal for the revision of the mff, which will be voted on tomorrow, preserves the principle of the unity of the union budget; upholds the treaties, providing that new programmes must be fully integrated into the union budget, respecting the community method; respects parliamentary democratic control; ensures the predictability of financing and multiannual programming and safeguards the transparency of budgetary decisions in the union; consolidates the principle that new initiatives require new resources; and provides that annual reinforcements are integrated into the mff through an adjustment to the fluctuation of the carbon price. one thing is clear, commissioner: we will not accept new programmes on an intergovernmental basis to the detriment of the community method. we will not accept funding for them from outside sources of funding. we will not accept the creation of new programmes at the expense of cuts in other key union programmes or policies, or in the financial envelopes of the member states. the citizens of the union must now, more than ever, be assured that the european union protects them and that the european parliament defends them, demanding greater transparency, more democracy. the european parliament will not allow its powers as budgetary authority to be weakened under any circumstances.
mr president, in june the second united nations conference on the oceans was held in lisbon. the international community has recognized the need for us to do more, to do better, to protect our oceans and our biodiversity. it is therefore disappointing that negotiations on the protection of biodiversity beyond national jurisdiction areas, which were aiming for an agreement this summer, have been inconclusive. the longer we delay in putting global measures into practice, the more we harm the most vulnerable, the most marginalised. leaders around the world, keep your eyes on the science and the appeals of young people at the high-level negotiations we'll be holding later this year. a coordinated response is imperative that recognizes the need to respect the limits of our planet earth. today, the crisis is not just one, it's a climate crisis, it's an environmental crisis, it's an ocean and biodiversity crisis.

mr president, brazil is a fundamental and important partner of the european union. however, brazil and its citizens are currently experiencing a dangerous and structuring economic, environmental and social crisis. and the first round of elections will take place on october 2nd, and i don't believe that it will be the populists, both left and right - and i'm talking about lula da silva and bolsonaro - who will be able to restructure that country and turn it into the nation it should be. and so we need to implement, i believe brazilians need to implement, a national development plan that has as its pillar education, the ecologically sustainable reindustrialization of their industry, that they can distribute wealth better, fight violence, fight climate crimes and ensure the projection of this country to the nation that it should be. i hope then that the next president of the federative republic of brazil who will speak to this house will be ciro gomes.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, when we talk about droughts and fires, we also talk about the growing impact of climate change on our lives, which is manifested in the increase in global temperatures, in the heat waves that repeatedly strike europe, or even in storms and floods, so often out of season. but let's not kid ourselves. climate change and its consequences are also largely responsible for the rise in food prices for europeans. and so, looking for solutions for agriculture that do not involve coordinated action to combat climate change and a more sustainable way of life will always be doomed to failure, failure that has also been the expression that applies to the action of many national governments, such as that of portugal, governments that for many years had the conditions and the money necessary to develop better solutions for forest fire prevention, monitoring and combat, but also water management and saving systems, and very little or nothing has been done. it's just that drought and firefighting have not been a priority for the various governments, specifically in portugal, and this summer is once again the result of that. the government has been negligent, i would even say incompetent, in keeping the forest and the people safe. i would like to thank the firefighters who often sacrifice their lives to compensate for the inaction of the authorities.
mr president, once again we are talking about drought and its serious impacts on society and ecosystems. and once again it seems to me that nothing structural will be done, because the majority of members, governments and european institutions continue to look the other way. to ensure the survival of humanity we must face reality. we need to be radical, that is, to get to the root of the problem. we need to preserve and regenerate our native forests, because without them there is no water intake. we need to change our model of food production and reduce our food's water footprint, because without that, we'll continue to waste water. and finally, we urgently need to change our eating habits, reducing and drastically cutting down on animal protein consumption, as this is one of the most inefficient ways of food production and has a huge water footprint.
mr president, commissioner, this debate here in the european parliament today is a tribute to all the firefighters and all the mayors who have fought and fought in adverse conditions. but it is also to say that if natural disasters increase in intensity and severity, europe and many of the member states must act more and more effectively. the commissioner says that it is important to have this consultation and i would also like to encourage him to do so. three very important notes: first, a strategy for integrated water management with appropriate instruments. we have to take advantage of the water that flows, much of which is wasted. second, a strategy for forest fires, but fundamentally prevention, prevention, prevention, prevention. this is the first step. many member states, and i am talking about my own country, do not use this very important mechanism, but then also, of course, concerted action. the third is solidarity. this is the time for europe to show its solidarity too, so that we cannot stand by idly when, for example, and i give the example of my own country, a natural park is burning, a large part of a natural park which is a unesco world heritage site, as happened in serra da estrela in portugal.
on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, the adoption of the minimum wage directive at european level is a major step in the right direction. i would like to congratulate the rapporteurs of the european parliament, in particular my colleague agnes jongerius, and the other colleagues who have worked and coordinated this matter, so that we have reached a point of no return, and indeed an acceleration. it is true that in many european countries we already have adequate minimum wages, but it is also true that steps are being taken in other countries to ensure a decent life for all workers. no one should live in poverty while working. you should have a life of dignity, and that is particularly more important at the present time. this is the time to speed up the implementation of this directive, not to wait for the transposition deadline. the member states must now act now, at a time when workers are suffering from the current energy price crisis. the workers who are paid the minimum wage must now also benefit from the great work done in the adoption of this directive.
i would like to thank the rapporteur, mr president, commissioner, colleagues, in particular for the political will to agree on a minimum wage directive, especially at this difficult time. it is not normal, we agree, that people on minimum wages in europe are not living on it, cannot meet their basic needs and end up in poverty. the current inflation, energy, food prices will further impoverish people on the minimum wage. the urgent implementation of this directive, involving the social partners, must therefore be a priority for the member states and we would ask the commission to monitor this process closely. this directive will ensure that the minimum income level corresponds to purchasing power, real subsistence levels, so that it is reviewed in a timely and regular manner. the minimum wage must ensure that people who receive it can live, support their families. only then will europe be socially strong, sustainable and resilient, especially in times of crisis.
mr president, commissioner, i have to ask you to stop this. when we started working on the directive on adequate minimum wages in the european union, we were in a different reality. and although we saw at that time that the union was particularly vulnerable to poverty and that there was a phenomenon of working poverty, we have never been more convinced than we are today of the need to act in the face of soaring prices and the energy crisis. from the outset, the aim of the directive was not to harmonise the minimum wage mechanism in the european union, but rather to set minimum requirements and promote collective agreements in order to strengthen and protect the minimum wage in the member states, to guarantee fair and adequate remuneration, and to contribute to poverty reduction. we all understand and respect the different traditions in the various member states. the agreement that is on the table today is a good and balanced proposal. i am glad that the text includes provisions to prevent excessive administrative burdens on businesses. i am convinced that the adoption of the directive will contribute to better protecting workers' rights and to increasing the adequacy of wages in the union.
mr president, dear all, this historic directive introducing an adequate minimum wage and strengthening collective bargaining in europe is a very important step towards a more social europe. and my political family, the socialists and democrats, we do not stand alone in this fight. we have the trade unions on our side, but not only them. we have the citizens on our side – in recent eurobarometer polls this is proven. and also the participants in the conference on the future of europe want a stronger social europe. some 24 million citizens will directly profit from this directive. and commissioner, you are right to mention the essential workers, many of them women, because they deserve it. let us not forget that adequate minimum wages, strong collective bargaining and strong trade unions are crucial elements for any recovery strategy. they are tools to fight against social dumping. they are tools to fight against wage dumping. and they are tools to reach upward convergence in europe. and all this is not only good for the people, but it is also good for the economy. now it is important that the member states will swiftly transpose and implement this directive. many thanks to all who participated in this important work.
mr president, commissioner, i would like to thank you for your time. those who work must be paid a wage that allows them to live a decent life. that's obvious, but not, unfortunately, as obvious as it seems. the relationship between wages and salaries and the relationship between wages and social transfers is often completely incomprehensible. that is why i support the idea of a minimum wage at european level. however, given the different economic environment and the different development of social dialogue in the member states, it is essential to leave the details, including the setting of the minimum wage, to the member states. we must be aware that the minimum wage is a starting point and affects all other wages as well as other transfers. it is therefore essential to be extremely careful not to put jobs-creating businesses at risk by making wrong decisions. and of course, a sensible government and a sensible social partners will make sure that they find solutions through dialogue that are good for everyone.
mr president, commissioner, i am really glad that we have a debate like this and that we are going to have a vote tomorrow. yes, they got poor, a lot of people got poor. the appropriate wage, obviously we have to see what is appropriate, because if in germany, the hourly wage is 12 euros, in romania it is three. but i must make one thing very clear. if a country opposes the introduction of a minimum wage, it means that it does not respect its workers. and we have a problem here, that we have the mobility of workers, and i want every worker, no matter in which country they work, to benefit from the existence of a collective agreement and the existence of a minimum wage. that is why i think we must vote tomorrow and i very much believe, commissioner, that we must also link this decision with measures to make the economy work, because these increases, which are now also energy, all costs, affect very much especially micro and small and medium-sized enterprises. that's why i think we need to not forget about economic measures, but we need to have this minimum wage and the protection of workers, no matter where they work.
mr president, this directive under discussion today is, in our view, a dangerous instrument for the general stagnation of wages and, consequently, of the national minimum wage. to limit the adequacy of wages to indicators, and to these indicators, means on the one hand to admit and accept that workers earning the minimum wage are condemned to a situation of poverty and, on the other hand, means to condition the future upward trend of the minimum wage to the evolution of the rest of the wage bill. it is true that the directive does not deprive governments of the ability to set the minimum wage they consider fair and adequate for living in dignity. but it is also true that it gives apparently technical reasons for the european commission, employers and their governments to try to prevent fair and dignified minimum wage increases in countries like portugal, where the government and employers' associations have been quick to claim that we have already met these indicators, 60% of the gross median wage and 50% of the gross median wage. but portuguese workers know that the minimum wage is far below the mark and that what is required is a general increase in wages and the national minimum wage.
on behalf of the s&d group, mr president, mr high representative, mr josep borrell, the recent visit of the president of the united states congress to taiwan has served as a pretext for the people's republic of china to make an unprecedented disproportionate show of force in the taiwan strait, with large-scale military exercises, including the launch of ballistic missiles that flew over taiwan. we have a very clear message from our political family to convey to the chinese authorities, and i think that it will also be very clear in the resolution of this parliament tomorrow: this attempt to intimidate is unacceptable and deserves our strongest condemnation. any unilateral attempt to change the status quo, to undermine stability in the region, would have the most serious consequences. one thing we know: china will play a big role in the world in the coming decades, that's for sure, but what role does china want to play? do you want to be on the side of cooperation, within the framework of international law, or do you want to be on the side of aligning with those who have been deteriorating world peace and order? that's what we're going to see in the next few days.
vice-president of the commission / high representative of the union for foreign affairs and security policy. – madam president, well i think that this debate confirms that we remain committed to our one-china policy. that we share the same interests in preserving peace and stability and the status quo in the taiwan strait, as it is indeed for the whole asia pacific region and for us. first
